| `Ska/CompUnproxy.lean` | The proxies.  `unproxyBlob cond`: a block with each proxy register read as its definition (`renBlob` of `unproxy`, labels kept, no chase); with no proxy it is the identity (`unproxyBlob_empty`).  `CondFresh g0 g1`: `cond` changed only at registers allocated in between, which `+proxy` meets (`proxy_condFresh`: the proxy is `g.re`, unproxied it reads as its definition) and which composes (`CondFresh.trans`); below `g0.re` unproxying does not change (`unproxy_condFresh`); `CondBelow`: the keys of `cond` are allocated registers.  `AgreeU cond`: `AgreeP` with the blocks outside the lazy blocks compared after `unproxyBlob cond`, the hop of a lazy block renamed too; with an empty `cond` it is `AgreeP` (`agreeU_empty`).  For the top: `+chase` on the renamed blocks is `+chase` followed by the renaming (`chase_ren`, under `RankDown`, the rank condition of `DownRk` without the bound on labels, which holds of the blocks of `+to-straight` with the start at `0`, `rankDown_start`), so `chaseFin_ren`, `keepBlock_ren`, and `renameBlocks_ren`: the renaming path by `ren ∘ unproxy cond`, which is what `toStraightF_run` gives with proxies, holds at each label the block that the renaming path by `ren` makes of the unproxied blocks, which is `Sim.kept` for the program of the unproxied blocks. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyEntry.lean` | The entry of `+to-straight` with proxies.  `toStraightF_entryU`: on a run of inputs, with or without proxy registers, if `toStraightF cond blocks ned o` returns `c'` and the blocks go down in a rank, entering `c'` on the arguments `split` takes out of `s` does what the unproxied start block `unproxyBlob cond start` does in a program `c` that holds the Gen blocks unproxied outside `o`, from registers that hold `s` as `ned` asks.  The simulation `Sim` is taken with the map of the unproxied blocks (`HoonMap.put (HoonMap.del c.blocks o) 0 (unproxyBlob cond start)`) and the injective `renReg`, `Sim.kept` from `renameBlocks_ren` with the rank of `rankDown_start`.  `proxRen_eq`: the renaming of the map path is `ren ∘ unproxy cond` in both cases (`hasProxies_none`: no proxy means an empty `cond`).  `FillSem.pair`: a fill from `ned` is a fill from `pair ned sur` holding `[s0 v]` when the registers of `sur` lie below the keep bound `lo`, so the input need of a run can be widened by registers of the run itself. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyRegs.lean` | The registers that ops mention (`poleRegs`, `RegsIn`).  Unproxying leaves ops alone whose registers are no keys of `cond` (`unproxy_ops`, from `renPole_fix`).  The ops of `+kern-need` mention only the source register, the registers of the target need and registers allocated in the loop (`kernLoop_regs`, `kernBoth_regs`).  The header records a check on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/selfkeep.py`): over 2930 runs of `run` (352 of which make a proxy), no register of a need of a run's result is a key of `cond`, the ops a run emits write no register of its result's sure need nor of its lazy entries' needs, and the sure need and the entries' needs share no register. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyFill.lean` | The registers the fill ops of `+coerce-lazy` mention.  `sureLoop_regs`: the ops of `sure-loop` from `ned` into `tgt` mention only registers of `ned`, of `tgt` and registers at or above the start's `re`.  `Front.uniq`: the ops in front of a block that exists are unique.  `coerceLazy_regs`: the ops of one coercion mention only registers of the input need, of the lazy need taken there and fresh registers (the target of `+sure-require-look` is made of the lazy need's registers and fresh ones).  `coerceList_regs`, `coerceLazyL_regs`: the same at the block of the `next` and at every entry of a lazy tree with distinct labels (`FillRegs`). |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyTop.lean` | The final pass for runs that make proxies.  `RunOKU`: `RunOKP` with `CondFresh g0 g1` in place of `cnd`, `AgreeU` by the run's final `cond` in `sem`, and no register of the sure need or of an entry's need a key of that `cond` (`ncond`, `econd`).  `RunOKP.toU`: a run without proxies from an empty `cond` meets it.  `mapVals`, `get_mapVals`: a map with every value mapped, over the keys.  `finishLF_compDoneU`, `finishLF_bellContractU`: the final pass with the whole of `+to-straight` (`toStraightF`) meets the relation at the entry and the contract, for any nomm whose run for `%done` from `gen0` meets `RunOKU`; the program it is compared with is the Gen blocks unproxied, and the fill ops are unchanged by the unproxying (`CondBelow`, `ncond`, `econd`, `coerceLazyL_regs`, `unproxy_ops`).  `finishLF_bellContractR_U`, `finishLF_bellContractXU`, `finishLF_bellContract71U`: the earlier top theorems through `RunOKU`. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyBelow.lean` | Fact (i5) and its use.  `JmpIn`, `FinIn`, `BlobIn`: every register of a jump, terminator or block satisfies `P`, so a renaming that agrees there renames the block alike (`renBlob_congr`).  `BelowIn lo g`: every block of `g` at a label in `[lo, g.bo)` mentions only registers below `g.re`; fact (i5) of a run from `g0` to `g1` is `BelowIn g0.bo g1`.  `agreeU_transfer`: on such blocks `AgreeU` by one `cond` is `AgreeU` by any `cond` that agrees with it below `g1.re`.  `RunOKU.semW`, `RunOKU.semF`: the `sem` of `RunOKU` at a later `cond` (one that agrees below `g1.re`, or the `cond` of a later state reached by `CondFresh`), given (i5).  The steps for the induction that is to prove (i5): `belowIn_start`, `BelowIn.mono`, `belowIn_re`, `belowIn_emitted`, `belowIn_emit` (when the run does not drop), `belowIn_addOps`, `belowIn_proxy`, `BelowIn.ext` (a sub-run that keeps the old blocks), `BelowIn.front` (ops put in front of one old block). |
| `Ska/CompBelowRun.lean` | Fact (i5) for every run of `run`, by induction on the nomm as `run_clean`.  The invariant is stronger than (i5): `Keeps g g'` says that every `BelowIn lo` of `g` holds of `g'` (for every `lo`), with `re` and `bo` grown and `drop` kept, so a sub-run composes with what comes before it whatever old blocks it changes.  `NB n ned`, `GoalIn n goal`, `NextIn n nex`: the registers of a need, of a goal (its need and the arguments of its jumps), of a `next` are below `n`.  The steps `keeps_re`, `keeps_emit`, `keeps_addOps`, and the arms `keeps_must`, `keeps_splitNeed`, `copyNeed_regs`, `keeps_copyNeed`, `keeps_sureRequireLook`, `keeps_crashNeed`, `keeps_medeLoop`, `keeps_mede`, `keeps_forl`, `keeps_doneBlocks`, `keeps_simpleNext`, `keeps_bomb`, `keeps_intoLoop`, `keeps_split`, `keeps_copy`, `keeps_into`, `keeps_collapseLazyAtom`.  `run_keeps`: every run of `run` from a state that does not drop, for a goal whose registers are below `re`, keeps every `BelowIn lo` and returns a `next` whose registers are below its end (every case: `%0`, `%1`, cons, `%3`, `%4`, `%5`, `%7`, `%10`); `run_below`: fact (i5) of the run. |
| `Ska/CompBelowX.lean` | Fact (i5) on `FragX`.  `KeepX nm`: every run of `runL nm` for a fork-free goal whose registers are below `re`, from a state that does not drop, when `nm` has at most 255 `%6`, meets `KeepR`: `Keeps`, the registers of the result (sure need, the needs of its lazy entries, `LazyIn`, and the jump's arguments, `NextInL`) below its end, `cond` kept, at most `2 * sixCount nm` entries, at labels allocated in the run.  The cases `keepX_frag` (through `run_keeps`), `keepX_sixP` (`%done`, `%pick`, through `sixBody_apart`), `keepX_sixN` (a fork-free `%next`, through `sixNextSimple_apart`, `forkSure_spec` and `sect_spec`: the branches keep `cond`, so `+sect` changes no block), `keepX_four`, `keepX_three`, `keepX_seven`, `keepX_cons`, `keepX_five`, `keepX_ten` (the copy through `copyL_apart_some`, `keep_copyL`), `keepX_sevenFrom`; `runX_keeps`, the induction; `runX_below`: (i5) on `FragX`; `runX_okU`: the top run for `%done` from `gen0` meets `RunOKU` and (i5), so `RunOKU.semW` and `RunOKU.semF` apply to it.  The 255 bound keeps every fork small, so `+lazy-shut` makes no proxy. |
| `Ska/CompFootRun.lean` | Fact (i) for `run`, the write footprint.  `poleWr` (the destination of an op), `OpsWr Q A`, `BlobWr Q b` (the parameters and destinations of a block satisfy `Q`), `WroteIn Q g g'` (every block of `g'` is the block of `g` with ops in front that write only `Q`, or a block that writes only `Q`), with `WroteIn.refl`, `.trans`, `.mono`, `wroteIn_re`, `wroteIn_emit`, `wroteIn_addOps`.  The footprints of the arms: `wr_splitNeed` (the need split), `wr_crashNeed`, `wr_medeLoop`, `wr_mede`, `wr_forl`, `wr_doneBlocks` (the goal's need and fresh registers, `NewOr`), `wr_simpleNext`, `wr_bomb` (nothing), `wr_copyNeed` (registers of the two needs and none of the copy, for distinct needs apart and below `re`, through `wr_two`), `wr_collapseLazyAtom` (the goal's need and fresh registers, and the register it returns).  `Foot g g' goal nex`: the run writes only registers below its end that it allocated or its goal needs and none of its result's sure need (`wr`), the sure need lies in those registers (`sure`) and is distinct (`dist`).  `FootIH`: every run from a state that does not drop, for a goal whose registers are below `re` and whose need is distinct (`GDist`), meets `Foot`.  Done: `foot_n0`, `foot_n1`, `foot_n7`, `foot_n4`, `foot_n3` (through `foot_tail`); `run_foot_of`: the induction, with cons, `%5` and `%10` as hypotheses (cons and `%5` are now `foot_cons` and `foot_n5` of `CompFootCons.lean`). |
| `Ska/CompFrame.lean` | The semantic frame lemma on the paths of `ExecPath.lean`.  `stepWr x`: the registers the next step writes (the block's parameters when it passes arguments, the destination `poleWr` of the op it runs, nothing for a jump).  `evalPass_frame`, `execPole_frame` (with `localPole_frame`): `+eval-pass` changes only the parameters, an op only its destination.  `step_frame`: a step leaves `HoonMap.get` unchanged outside `stepWr x`, so a value stays that value and poison stays poison.  `steps_frame`: when every block at a label of `L` writes only registers of `Q` (`BlobWr Q`) and takes only jumps into `L` (`ProgIn`, `TakenIn`), and so does what is left of the block of `x` (`CfgIn`), every path from `x` keeps the registers outside `Q` and enters only labels of `L`; `steps_frame_all` with every label.  `run_frame`, `execEntry_frame`: the same for the path of a run that returns (`run_iff_path`, `execEntry_iff_path`); `steps_reads_frame`: on such a path a real read of a register outside `Q` reads the value it had at the start. |
| `Ska/CompFootCons.lean` | Fact (i) for cons and `%5`.  `copyNeed_dist`: the copy of two distinct needs apart and below `re` is distinct, with no `Clean`.  `wr_splitNeedS`, `split_foot`: `+split` writes only registers of the goal's need that are in neither half, and the halves are distinct, apart, and made of the goal's registers and fresh ones.  `foot_copyTail`: the run of `q`, the run of `p` and the `+copy` of the two results, from the footprints of the two runs (the code of `p` writes no register of `q`'s result).  `foot_cons`, `foot_n5`: the cases of `run_foot_of`, for every goal.  `%10` is in `CompFootTen.lean`. |
| `Ska/CompFootTen.lean` | Fact (i) for `[10 [a v] q]`, so for every run of `run`.  `intoRec_wr`: the `%con` ops of the walk of `+into-need` write registers of the need being taken apart or registers allocated in the walk, and none of the donor's or the recipient's need (the op of a step writes the top register of a `%this` or `%both`, which is in neither half, and the deeper ops write the fresh registers the step made for the half on the path); `intoNeed_wr`, `into_wr`: the same through the loop's one `+add-ops` and the block `+into` emits.  `foot_n10`: `+simple-next`, `+into`, then `foot_copyTail` with the donor `v` run first.  `run_foot`: `FootIH` of every nomm (`run_foot_of` with `foot_cons`, `foot_n5`, `foot_n10`). |
| `Ska/CompFootX.lean` | Fact (i) over `FragX`, with the lazy entries.  `FootR g0 g1 goal nex`: the code writes only registers below the end that the run allocated or the goal needs, none of the result's sure need and none of its entries' needs (`wr`, facts (i) and (iii)); the sure need and every entry's need lie in those registers (`sure`, `esure`) and are distinct (`dist`, `edist`); the sure need shares no register with an entry's need (`apart`, fact (iv)).  `FootX nm`: every run of `runL nm` for a fork-free goal whose registers are below `re` and whose need is distinct, from a state that does not drop, with at most 255 `%6`, meets it.  `FootR.pre`: code in front of a run that writes no register of the run's goal or allocations, with a wider goal.  The cases, mirroring `CompBelowX.lean`: `footX_frag` (from `run_foot`), `footX_four`, `footX_three` (through `footX_tail`), `footX_seven`, `footX_sevenFrom`, `footX_cons` (through `footX_consBody`), `footX_five`, `footX_ten`, the last three through `footX_binary` (the two runs and `+copy-lazy` of their results, whose entries lie apart, stated for the goal whose need is the pair of the operands' needs) and `foot_copy` (the `+copy` of two distinct sure needs).  `footX_sixP`: `[6 c a b]` under `%done` and `%pick` (the branches write registers they allocate, the test registers above both, `sixGenL` nothing, the `+copy` of the test's need with the two lazy blocks the registers of the test's need; the entries are the test's, the two branches' lazy needs and their entries).  `footX_sixN`: `[6 c a b]` under a fork-free `%next` (`+fork-sure` writes only the join's parameters, the goal's need, through `wroteIn_forkSure`; the branches write registers they allocate or the copies `s1` and `s0`, which are fresh and apart; `+sect` changes no block, `wroteIn_sameBlocks`).  `runX_foot_of`: the induction over `FragX` with that case as its hypothesis, and `runX_foot`: fact (i) over `FragX` with no hypothesis. |
| `Ska/CompLeafKeep.lean` | The leaf conversion with the relaxed input hypothesis (README Gaps 12.2).  `Clear g`: no block at or above `g.bo`; `clear_gen0`, and `Clear.grow`, `Clear.growU` keep it; `clear_new`, `clear_newU`: from a clear state every block of the end state at a label from the start's `bo` on was made by the run, so it writes only the footprint and jumps below its label (or as `JumpsU` says).  `steps_transfer`: a path of one program is a path of every program with the same blocks at the labels it enters.  `steps_exit`: the frame lemma up to the first exit, for blocks at labels of `L` that write only `Q` and may jump out of `L` (`WrIn`); its exit case returns the configuration before the exit jump, the jump and the block it enters.  `regionProg`: the program of the proof, with the blocks of a state at the labels of a range and one block at the goal's label, and no other block (`regionProg_in`, `regionProg_z`, `regionProg_out`, through `putFrom`).  `run_jump`: a configuration at its terminator runs as `goJ` of the jump it takes.  `leaf_exit`: if entering `je` does what entering a jump `j` without arguments below `g0.bo` does from some register file in every `regionProg` at `j.there`, then one register file `R'`, equal to the start outside `Q`, does it in every program that agrees with the run (the programs with `bomB` and `zeroB` at `j.there` exclude an end inside the run and fix the state and the trace).  `holdsN_pointwise`: a need held register by register by several files is held by one.  `leaf_sem` and `leaf_keep`: from `RunOK`, `Grow`, `Foot` and `Clear g0`, for a goal whose jumps pass no arguments and go below `g0.bo` (`LeafJ`), the code keeps the registers below `g0.re`, from `g1.re` on and of the result's sure need, outside the goal's need; for `%next` the need is held by `R'` through one program `⟨[], [], don r⟩` per register `r`. |
| `Ska/CompLeafRel.lean` | The relaxed `sem` (README Gaps 12.2).  `InR goal g0 g1 sur r`: the input registers a run may be given, from its end on, below its start and not named by its goal, or in its result's sure need `sur`; `KeepI goal g0 ned r`: what the code keeps, the registers below the start not named by the goal and every input register.  `SemPR` (`%done`, `%pick`), `SemGR` (any goal) and `SemNR` (`SemGR` at `%next`): the `sem` fields with `InR` as the hypothesis on `ned` and, for `SemGR`, `KeepI` as the keep set; `SemPR.strict` and `SemGR.strict` give the strict forms back.  The form planned first, `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` with the old keep set, does not compose through two operands: in `[5 [0 2] [0 3]]` the whole's sure need is `pair rp rq`, and the `sem` of `[0 2]` would be asked for `rq`, below its start and outside its sure need `pair rp none`; in `[5 [0 1] [1 6]]` the operand `[0 1]` runs for `%next rp` with `rp` in `ned`, which the old keep set drops.  `leaf_keepK`: the leaf conversion `leaf_sem` with the footprint `run_foot` for any keep set outside the footprint.  `runOKP_frag_semR`, `runOKN_frag_semR`: the relaxed `sem` at the leaves, from `Clear g0` and `LeafJ`. |
| `Ska/CompRelSix.lean` | The relaxed `sem` over `FragP` and `FragN`, proved beside the strict relations (README Gaps 12.2).  The relations keep their strict `sem`, because the leaf conversion and the footprints come after the files that build them in the import order (`CompLeafKeep.lean` imports `CompNestPick.lean`, `CompFootX.lean` imports `CompBinaryN.lean` and `CompFragM.lean`); the relaxed `sem` is proved by the same induction from the relations of the parts and the relaxed `sem` of the parts.  `OKPR`, `OKNR`: what the induction proves, `OKP` and the hypotheses of `runN_ok` with `LeafJ`, `Clear g0` and `SemPR`, `SemNR` as the conclusion.  `sixP_semR`: `[6 c a b]` for `%done` and `%pick`, the `sem` part of `sixP_ok` with the relaxed hypothesis passed on (the test gets it as it is, the lazy blocks of the branches because the test's sure need lies from `g2.re` on, `Rc.regs`).  `lazyBlkNR`, `sixNext_semR`: the same under a fork-free `%next`, where the input registers also avoid the goal's need (`dsn`).  `runP_semR`, `runN_semR`: every run on `FragP` and `FragN`. |
| `Ska/CompRelCopy.lean` | The copy block keeps the merged need (README Gaps 12.2, a step toward `binaryN_ok` with the relaxed `sem`).  `holdsN_some`: a register of a held need holds a value.  `execOps_don_zero`, `runs_read`: a run of ops (`Runs`) that does not write a register keeps it, when it holds a value after the ops.  `copy_okR`: `copy_ok` with the clause that the copy block changes no register of the merged need (from `wr_copyNeed`: the ops write only registers of the two needs outside the merged one), which `binaryN_ok` needs when the input registers lie in the merged need, as `rq` of `[5 [0 2] [0 3]]` may. |
| `Ska/CompRelBinary.lean` | The relaxed `sem` through two operands, a unary operand and `%7` (README Gaps 12.2).  `KeepB`: `Keep` and the registers of the two operands' needs, the keep set the block lemmas must give.  `binaryN_semR`: `SemGR` of the shape of `binaryN_ok` from `RunOKN` and `SemNR` of the two operands and a block lemma with `KeepB`; the input registers go through the copy block by `copy_okR`, to `p`'s run as its sure need, as below its start outside `lp` (when in `q`'s sure need) or as above its end, and to `q`'s run a register of `lp` as below its start outside `lq`.  `n5Pre_okK`: the block of `%5` with `KeepB` (under `%next` it stores only into the collapse register and a fresh one).  `SemGR.toP`.  `runOKG_n5_semR`: `[5 p q]` for any fork-free goal, given `OKNR` of the operands and `Clear`.  `split_okK`: `split_ok` whose block keeps the head and tail registers (`wr_splitNeedS`, `runs_read`).  `consN_semR`: cons under a fork-free `%next`.  `unaryN_semR`: the shape of `unaryN_ok` with a block that keeps the operand register.  `runOKN_n7_semR`, `runOKP_n7g_semR`: `[7 p q]` with `q` a leaf, from `OKNR` of `p` and `leaf_keepK` of `q`. |
| `Ska/CompRelUnary.lean` | The relaxed `sem` of `[4 p]` and `[3 p]` for every goal (README Gaps 12.2).  `unaryG_semR`: the shape of `unaryG_ok` for `%done` and `%pick`, from `SemNR` of `p` and a block that keeps the operand register `a` besides `Keep`, the counterpart of `unaryN_semR`.  `runOKN_n4_semR`, `runOKN_n3_semR` (with `semN_n3_of_none` for the branch where the goal asks nothing of the product), `runOKP_n4_semR`, `runOKP_n3_semR` (`%done`), `runOKP_n4p_semR`, `runOKP_n3p_semR` (`%pick`): the blocks of the strict proofs with the keep set extended by `a`, since in `[4 [0 1]]` the input need may hold `a`, which the increment block does not write (it stores only into the product register). |
| `Ska/CompRelCons.lean` | Cons under `%done` and `%pick` and `[7 [0 axe] q]` with the relaxed `sem` (README Gaps 12.2).  `consN_semRK`: `consN_semR` as `OKNR`.  `runOKP_consD_semR`: under `%done`, the goal `%next (lazyReg r)` of the `%don` block, through `unaryG_semR` with the identity.  `runOKP_consP_semR`: under `%pick`, through `binaryN_semR` with the goal `%pick` and the `%bom` block.  `semGR_from`, `semPR_from`: `[7 [0 axe] q]` from the relaxed `sem` of `q`, whose sure-need registers are those of the whole (`fromSure_spec`), so `InR` carries over. |
| `Ska/CompRelTen.lean` | `[10 [a v] q]` with the relaxed `sem` for every goal (README Gaps 12.2).  `n10N_semR`: the proof of `n10N_body` with `binaryN_semR` and the block of `into_okK`, whose keep clause gives `KeepB` (the recipient's need as `lp`, the donor's as `lq`).  `runOKN_n10_semR` (`OKNR`), `runOKP_n10D_semR`, `runOKP_n10P_semR` (through `unaryG_semR`). |
| `Ska/CompRelX.lean` | The relaxed `sem` over `FragX` (README Gaps 12.2).  `OKXR`: `OKPR` for `%done`, `OKNR`, and `OKPR` for every `%pick` whose jumps pass no arguments.  `runX_semR`: every nomm of `FragX` meets `OKXR`, by the induction of `runX_ok`, every case, with no hypothesis (the strict relations of the parts from `runX_ok`).  `runX_semR_gen0`: the run for `%done` from `gen0` meets `SemPR`.  The top theorems are unchanged: they still use the strict `sem`, which `SemPR.strict` recovers. |
| `Ska/KnowSound.lean` | `knowGo_sound`: the nomm `+know` folds under a bake stands for the same formula on the subjects that fit both the bake and the code requirement, and its product sock fits every product. Every fold drops only a safe computation. `know_annot_of_implies`: on every subject of the bake, if the bake implies the code requirement. |
| `Ska/BakeGraph.lean` | A model of `+bake-graph`, arm by arm. `bakeLoop_sound`: at the end of the worklist, every site that `+pick` sends to no existing variant fits its callee's bake. `variant_annot`: a variant's nomm stands for its code bell's formula on the subjects of its bake and code requirement. `bakeOf` has the guard of commit 8f2162e (the bake is `less.c` when `+huge:so less.c both` says no), so every bake implies its code requirement (`bakes_ok`, `BakeImp.lean`), and the composition across pokes is `skaPokeB_sound` (`PokeBake.lean`). |
| `Ska/CompNestCoerce.lean`, `Ska/CompNest.lean` | Every lazy block of a lazy need, at any depth, in the order `+coerce-lazy` visits them (`entries`); `coerceLazyL_spec`: each gets ops in front that fill its need from the input registers, and no other block changes. Below `+lazy-max`, `+lazy-size` is one more than the number of entries. |
| `Ska/CompNestPick.lean`, `Ska/CompNestFinalP.lean` | `FragP`: `%6` nested in branches and in tests to any depth, under `%done` and `%pick`. `finishLF_bellContractP` and `crash_iffP`: for such a nomm with at most 255 `%6` (so `+lazy-shut` never fires and no proxy is made), the final pass meets the contract and the code crashes exactly when the nomm does. The final pass goes through `toStraight_entryR` with the rank `upRk`, and `finishLF_bellContractR`, `crash_iffR` state the same for any nomm whose run for `%done` meets `RunOKP`. (`CompNestRun.lean` and `CompNestFinal.lean` hold an earlier, narrower version and helpers.) |
| `Ska/BakeImp.lean` | When a bake implies its code requirement: `uniSock_left`, `purr_sub`, `normSockH_rev`, and an invariant of the worklist. `bakeOf_ok`: with the guard of commit 8f2162e every new bake is `BakeOK` (under `less.c`, and with a noun when it is not `less.c`: `uniSock_void`, `normCD_inhab`), and `bakes_ok`: every bake at the end of the worklist is `BakeOK`, with no hypothesis.  `bakes_ok_of_meet` (from `SitesMeet`, false on the real compiler, `SitesMeetFalse.lean`) is now a corollary that does not use its hypothesis; before the guard its conclusion was false too and the compiler miscompiled (`VariantCollision.lean`). The header explains, from the Hoon, why two identities of one code bell are rare within a poke (only through the `.indi` test, a `%he` generalization, or an incomplete `+huge:so`), and records a check of `SitesMeet` on the real compiler: 11,370 sites under 3,033 bakes after fifteen pokes up to `(ream '(add 1 2)')`, with no failure. |
| `Ska/PokeBake.lean` | `+ska-poke` with `+bake-graph`, `dat`, `new-code` and the code table. `skaPokeB_sound`: the invariant `InvB` is kept across pokes, so every bell of the real code table agrees with `+mink` on every subject that fits it.  `BakesImply` holds of every `+bake-graph` by the guard of commit 8f2162e (`bakesImply`), so the hypothesis `himp` is gone (before the guard it was false, `SitesMeetFalse.lean`, and its failure miscompiled, `VariantCollision.lean`).  `InvB.varsSub`: every variant `vars.lon` lists under a code bell has a bake under that bell's code requirement.  `InvB.variant_agree`: a variant listed under a code bell `c` has `c`'s formula, and on every subject of its bake, which fits `less.c`, its entry agrees with `+mink` on `c`'s formula; `InvB.collide`: two code bells that list one variant share that one entry and it is right for both (the variant key `[bake fol]` needs no code bell, since `CodeOK` states the contract of an entry on its own bake). |
| `Ska/PokeAna.lean` | `+ska-poke` as the analysis arm of `+poke` (`anaArm`, `skaPokeB` on `[&+sub fol]`). `AnaHyp` from `SitesMeet` of every poke (`anaHyp_of_meet`). The root's code requirement holds of the subject (`skaPoke_root_fits`). `pokeS_sound_of_meet`, `pokeS_invKept_of_meet` and `gap_of_meet` need only `CompHyp` and `PokeSites`, and `anaHyp_of_bakes` needs `PokeBakes`; both were false of the Hoon before commit 8f2162e (`SitesMeetFalse.lean`, `VariantCollision.lean`).  With the guard `PokeBakes` always holds (`pokeBakes`), so `anaHyp_of_arm`, `pokeS_sound_of_comp`, `pokeS_invKept_of_comp` and `gap_of_comp` need only `CompHyp`.  The root's code requirement holds of every subject of `root.1` by `app_fits`, whatever cape is united with `want` (1813f09). |
| `Ska/PokeHalt.lean` | `FullAnswers` from `CompHyp` and one hypothesis per loop (`AnaEnds`, `BakeEnds`, `RootAnswers`; `fullAnswers_of_loops`; `PokeSites` is no longer needed since the guard of commit 8f2162e). The final pass never crashes (`finalEnds_always`). The worklist does not depend on extra fuel (`bakeLoop_mono`), and bakes only lose knowledge (`bakeLoop_grows`). Not proved: that the worklist and the analysis end. |
| `Ska/BakeMeasure.lean` | The normal form of `+norm-sock` (`CanonCD`, `normCD_canon`, `normCD_of_canon`) and a measure `kap` on it: on normal socks, a strict loss of nouns lowers it (`kap_sub`, `kapS_lt`), and the same nouns give the same sock (`canon_ext`).  Every bake at the end of the worklist is `less.c` or normal (`bakes_canon`), and a change of a bake that keeps its nouns goes to or from `less.c` (`noLoss_lessc`).  `less.c` is often not normal on the real compiler (2,045 of 3,033 pairs checked), so `BakeEnds` still needs a bound on the changes back to `less.c`. |
| `Ska/SitesMeet.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `SitesMeet` from three smaller invariants (`sitesMeet_of_parts`): `LessProvides` (under the code requirement itself, every site provides its callee's code requirement), `KnowMono` (`+know` is monotone in the subject sock: under a bake below `less.cur` each site has a site of the same callee under `less.cur` that knows no more; false for the model in general, see `KnowMonoFalse.lean`) and `SitesInhab` (no site's sock is `%void`).  `pokeSites_of_parts`: `PokeSites` from the three for every poke.  None of the three is proved; all three held on the real compiler on the 11,370 sites of `sitesmeet2.hoon` (3,033 pairs of a code bell and a bake, 1,555 of them strict) with no failure (`tools/hoon/sitesmeet3.hoon`).  `LessProvides` reduces (an argument in the header) to the coherence of the products of the identities of one code bell. |
| `Ska/KnowMonoFalse.lean` | Obsolete for good since the guard of commit 8f2162e (a part of the `SitesMeet` route).  `knowMono_false`: `KnowMono` fails for every folding environment whose `dat` has two given datums, so it cannot come from the sock operations of `+know` alone.  Under the bake a read folds and loses its provenance, a call on the other branch of a `%6` keeps provenance `2`, and `+double-int` forgets the part (`|`), while under `less.cur` neither folds and the join keeps `[& |]` `[7 0]`.  `SitesMeet` is not refuted by it (the site knows `|`).  Not checked on the real compiler. |
| `Ska/SitesMeetNF.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `sitesMeet_of_nf`: `SitesMeet` from `LessProvidesNF` (under `less.cur`, `+know` without folding lists sites that provide their callees' code requirements) and `KnowMeetsNF` (each site under a bake shares a noun with a site of the same callee of that run without folding), with no `SitesInhab`.  `+knit:so`, `+double-int` and `+pull:so` keep `Meets` (`meets_knit`, `meets_doubleInt`, `meets_doubleInt_left`, `meets_pull`).  Both invariants held on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/sitesmeet4.hoon`: 11,370 sites under 3,033 bakes, 11,661 sites without folding, no failure). |
| `Ska/KnowMeets.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `knowMeetsNF_of`: `KnowMeetsNF` from `DatInhab` (datum products have a noun), `LessInhab` (code requirements of datums have a noun) and `NoAx0Dat` (no `%10` at axis `0`), all three checked on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/sitesmeet5.hoon`).  By induction on the nomm over the run under the bake and the run without folding under `less.cur` (`knowGo_meets`, every case), with `+patch-prod` and `+darn:so` keeping `Meets` (`meets_patchProd`, `meets_darn` through `forceEdit`), literals knowing exactly their noun (`knowGo_lit`), and the run without folding never crashing (`knowGo_nf_some`).  `sitesMeet_of_lessNF`: `SitesMeet` from `LessProvidesNF` and the three facts, and `pokeSites_of_nf`: `PokeSites` from them for every poke (`PokeNF`).  The three facts hold of every poke from `InvB` (`poke_datFacts`, from `skaPoke_dat` and the memo invariant `PokeInv.dat`), so `PokeSites` needs only `LessProvidesNF` of every poke (`PokeLessNF`, `pokeSites_of_lessNF`), which is false on the real compiler for some pokes (`tools/hoon/voidscc.hoon`, see `SiteCover.lean`), so this route to `PokeSites` is closed.  The residue of `LessProvidesNF` as `ProdSame` (two identities of one code bell have the same patched products; `ProdMeet` is the weaker form), not proved to imply it; no two such identities occurred in the fifteen pokes checked, and on seven pokes built to make them by an `.indi` miss (`tools/hoon/prodsame.hoon`, 8 groups) `ProdSame` (under `less.c`), `ProdMeet`, `LessProvidesNF` and `SitesMeet` held with no failure. |
| `Ska/LessProvides.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `lessProvidesNF_of_cover`: `LessProvidesNF` from `SiteCover` (every site of `+know` without folding under `less.cur` has a sock `ka` with a noun that provides the callee's code requirement and knows, on that requirement's cape, no more than the site), through `sub_app_cape` (a sock with a noun under `[cs ds]`, masked by `cs`, is still under `[cs ds]`; for example `&+[5 7]` under `[[& |] [5 0]]` masks to `[[& |] [5 7]]`) and `sub_of_cover`; `pokeSites_of_cover`: `PokeSites` from `SiteCover` of every poke (`PokeSiteCover`).  `SiteCover` is not proved.  The header says why the split into a replay of the analysis' walk as `+know` with a table `datA` and a comparison of two walks was dropped: one nomm can call two identities of one code bell with different products (the `.indi` miss of `prodsame.hoon`), so `datA` must be indexed by the site, and the comparison needs `ProdSame` at the site socks that occur and the provenance fact that `want` holds what each callee reads.  Inlining needs no case (an inlined call is a `%7` of the nomm).  `ProdSame` at the site socks that occur (the sites of `+know` without folding under `less.cur` and the callees' subject socks) held on the pokes of `prodsame.hoon` and `he2.hoon` (`tools/hoon/prodsites.hoon`: 490 site socks, 6,848 comparisons, no failure); the header ends with a plan for `SiteCover` (`knowT`, `Replay`, `Compare`). |
| `Ska/SiteCover.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  Refuted on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/voidscc.hoon`, `voidSccWitness`): `SiteCover`, so `Compare` and `LessProvidesNF`, are false for a poke whose root's SCC has a member that never returns.  The root's last run reads that member's product `%void` and keeps the other branch of a `%6`, while `dat` has the `|+~` that `+pop` makes of it, so `+know` without folding forgets the branch: one site, whose callee requires `&+[1 7]`, has `|+~`.  `KnowMeetsNF` and `SitesMeet` hold there.  The header says why the five formulas of `voidcall.hoon` make no `%void` and what a comparison that can hold would walk (the analysis' table with `%void` kept).  `knowT`: `+know` without folding and with `idc`, its call products read from a table `PTab` indexed by the sites so far, the callee bell and the site sock.  `+know false` with `dat` is the instance `datTab` (`knowT_datTab`, every case; `know_sites_knowT`).  `siteCover_of`: `SiteCover` from `Replay` (every datum has a `knowT` walk of its nomm under a subject sock `more` with `less.cur = app want more`, whose non-root sites have socks with a noun under their callees' code requirements) and `Compare` (each site of the walk under `less.cur` covers a site of the same callee in that walk on the callee's code cape); `lessProvidesNF_of_replay`.  Neither is proved, and no case of `Compare` is.  `ProdSameM`: `ProdSame` with equal maps (`prodSame_of_M`), which `tools/hoon/prodmaps.hoon` supports: 490 site socks, 6,848 comparisons, 0 maps differ; at 230 callee identities the unpatched product used by the analysis has the nouns and map of itself and of `dat`'s patched for its subject sock. |
| `Ska/SitesMeetFalse.lean` | Refuted on the real compiler before commit 8f2162e (`tools/hoon/bakedrop.hoon`, `bakeDropWitness`): `SitesMeet`, so `PokeSites`, and `BakesImply`, so `PokeBakes`.  On the changed compiler `SitesMeet` still fails at the one site, but the guard gives `H`'s variant the code requirement itself (`bakedrop.hoon`: `bad=0` of 7 variants, 2026-11-24), `BakesImply` is proved (`bakesImply`) and nothing needs `SitesMeet` any more.  The root's SCC has a member `F` that never returns.  The analysis reads `F`'s product `%void`, so a branch `[[[3 0] 5] F]` of a `%6` is `%void` and `+double-int` keeps the other branch `[[[1 7] 5] 9]`, and the callee `H = [2 [0 1] [0 4]]` requires axis `4` to be `[1 7]`.  `+know` under the bake folds the literal test to the first branch, where `F`'s product is the `|+~` of `+pop`, so `H`'s site knows axis `4` is `[3 0]`: no common noun (`witness_no_meet`, proved).  `+uni-sock` keeps the fully known side, so `H`'s variant gets a bake that is not under its code requirement (1 bad pair).  `KnowMeetsNF` holds there.  Within this poke the code is right (the site follows a call that never returns), but every route to `SitesMeet` is closed, including a reference walk that keeps the analysis' `%void` products.  A later poke makes the compiled code wrong (`VariantCollision.lean`). |
| `Ska/VariantCollision.lean` | Fixed by the guard of commit 8f2162e: on the changed compiler `collide.hoon` agrees (`[%after-witness %same]`, 2026-11-24), and `InvB.collide` (`PokeBake.lean`) proves a shared variant right for both code bells.  The record of the bug: a miscompile on the real compiler before the fix (`tools/hoon/collide.hoon`): after the poke of `bakeDropWitness`, the formula `[7 [[1 [[3 0] 5]] (dec 40)] [2 [0 1] [1 H]]]` on the library gives `[%0 7]` under `+eval-top` and `[%2 ~]` under `+mock` (alone the two agree, `collide0.hoon`).  `H`'s new code bell (axis `4` is `[3 0]`) bakes to the same sock as the dead variant of the first poke (`collide_same_bake`), variants are keyed by `[bake fol]`, and `new-code` skips a key the table has, so the live call runs the dead variant's nomm `[7 [0 1] [1 7]]` (`collide-vars.hoon` prints both code bells listing the one variant).  No invariant with a domain per bell repairs this.  The fix in the Hoon: use `less.c` as the bake when `(huge:so less.c bake)` fails; then `BakesImply` holds by construction and `GoodSite` survives the fallback (`bakeGuard_fits`).  Or key variants by their code bell too. |
| `Ska/AnaFuel.lean` | Fuel monotonicity of the analysis: whatever `folLoop`, `resolve`, `analyze`, `run`, `passLoop`, `memberLoop` return at fuel `n` they return at every fuel `m ≥ n` (`fuelStep`, one unit for the six at once by induction on `n`; `folLoop_fuel_le`, `resolve_fuel_le`, `resolveR_fuel_le`, `analyze_fuel_le`, `run_fuel_le`, `passLoop_fuel_le`, `memberLoop_fuel_le`), from steps monotone in the arms they call (`folStep_le`, `resolveStep_le`, `analyzeStep_le`, `runStep_le`, `passStep_le`, `memberStep_le`) and the loops with their own fuel (`sweepLoop_fuel`, `liveLoop_fuel`, `cull_fuel`, `pruneLoop_fuel`); and `skaCallgraph_fuel_le`, `skaPoke_fuel_le`.  The order `OLe` is `Below?` of `Iter.lean` unfolded, so the file does not import the executor.  `allP` of `AnaInv.lean` does not give this (it carries a property of each result). |
| `Ska/AnaEnds.lean` | Termination of the analysis.  The header gives the Hoon's argument loop by loop: `fol-loop` is structural up to `%8`, `%9` and one level of inlining (an inlineable formula has no `%2`), proved for the formulas that do not inline (`FolWeight.lean`); a `%he` turn of `resolve` strictly cuts the subject sock (`cape-size` plus leaves `&`), proved (`HeTurn.lean`, `HeBound.lean`); the stack is finite because the callable formulas (cape exactly `&`, which `+knit:so` never makes) are finitely many and `+he-sock` is Kruskal's embedding over finitely many leaves, argued, not proved, with no bound; the pass loop's measure since 1813f09 would be, per entry, the cape size of `less-code` (which only grows inside the cape of `bus`, `runDatum_less_mask`), the knowledge of the product (which only shrinks), then `indi` and `seek`, with the provenance `map` and a culled member found again as the open points; not proved.  `AnaEnds` may be false as stated: `InvB` says nothing of the callees of memo entries, and a memo hit with a callee outside the graph makes `+prune-callgraph` crash.  `MemoNoCallees` (every memo the model makes has it, `memoNoCallees_skaPoke`); `pruneLoop_ends`, `pruneCallgraph_ends`: the loop ends on a graph closed under callees within the queue's length plus `pruneLeft`; `anaHalts_of`: `AnaHalts` from `CgHalts` (`+ska-callgraph` returns at some fuel, not proved) and `GraphClosed` (its graph holds the root and is closed under callees), which is proved (`graphClosed`, from `skaCallgraph_closed` of `GraphClose.lean`), so `anaHalts_of_cgHalts` gives `AnaHalts` from `CgHalts` alone; `anaEndsN_of_halts` at `anaFuel`, `anaEndsN_mono`, `anaEnds_of_N`.  Measured on 19 pokes (`he2.hoon`, `sitesmeet4.hoon`, `voidscc.hoon`): at most 6 passes, stack depth 331, 32 frames of one formula, one `%he` turn. |
| `Ska/PassLoop.lean` | **The pass loop of `+analyze` did not end on `tools/hoon/swaploop.hoon` up to 1813f09**, and now returns: the function `[&+[F [1 0] [1 0]] F]` with `F = [6 [3 0 6] [0 6] [2 [1 0] [2 [[0 2] [0 7] [0 6]] [0 2]]]]` calls itself with its sample swapped (the same identity) and runs that call's product as a formula; once the product is `|+~` that call is indirect, and `less-code`, which `+run` then recomputed without the old entry, alternated between `[& [& |]]` and `[& [| &]]` through the swap (53,345 passes in 60 s on an instrumented copy, no answer after 300 s), so `CgHalts`, `AnaEnds` and `Halting` were false of that compiler.  Since 1813f09 `less-code` is `(app:ca (uni:ca want old) bus)` (`runDatum_less`), its mask only grows (`runDatum_less_mask`), a change of it still sets `changed` (`runDatum_changed_of_less`), and the poke returns on the compiler of this pill (`runhoon.sh swaploop.hoon`, 2026-11-24, `[%poke-returned %.y]`).  What is monotone: a run only loses knowledge of the product (`runDatum_prod_old`, from `mergeProd_fits_old` and `msgAnno_fits_right'`), and the mask of `less-code` only grows.  The header says what measure a proof of `CgHalts` would use (not attempted).  On the 19 pokes of `he2.hoon`, `sitesmeet4.hoon`, `voidscc.hoon` (per-pass comparison of every member's entry, on the compiler before 1813f09): `less-code`, `indi`, `seek` only grew, products only lost knowledge, no member was culled, and the member set changed after the first pass only by 51 new members. |
| `Ska/HeTurn.lean` | The `%he` lemma: `msgSock_he_lt` (if `heSock a b` and `a ≠ b`, then `msgSock a b` has a smaller `heMeasure`, the size of the cape plus its number of leaves `&`, than `a`) and so `msgSock_ne_of_he` (`msgSock a b ≠ some a`), from `knit_eq_cell`, `heSock_size` (`+he-sock` never grows the cape), `msgSock_cases` (the result is `|+~` or the knit of the results on the halves of two cells over cells), `msgSock_le` (the result's cape is no larger than either sock's, with no more leaves `&` than the first's) and `msgSock_left_size` (if the result is `a`, the cape of `b` is no smaller).  For example `a = [[& |] [5 ~]]` and `b = &+[5 6]` give `|+~`, measure `4` to `1`. |
| `Ska/HeBound.lean` | The `resolve` loop makes boundedly many `%he` turns: `recLoop_he` (a `%he` answer of `+recursive-call` comes from a frame of the same formula whose subject sock `+he-sock` embeds), `recursiveCall_he_lt` (for an identity off the stack the new subject sock has a smaller `heMeasure`), `HeTurn` (a `%he` turn of the loop begun with `orig`: the memo lookup at `orig.2` and the current sock missed), `resolve_heTurn` (a turn is one unit of fuel in the same state), `heTurn_lt` (with `env.he = heSock` and `SInv`, a turn cuts `heMeasure`), and `resolve_he_bound`, `resolve_he_bound'`: from `i` the loop reaches, after `k ≤ heMeasure i.1 ≤ 2 * sockSize i.1` turns `%he`, an identity whose turn is not `%he`, and the loop at `i` with fuel `n + k` is the loop there with fuel `n`. |
| `Ska/FolWeight.lean` | `fol-loop` within one run, with the direct calls fixed (`folLoopR env res`).  `folW`: a weight that counts `%8`, `%9`, `%11` above the `%7` and `%2` forms they become.  `NoInl`: the calls that never inline (an inlineable formula, or `[2 [0 1] x]` with `x` inlineable and `bare-call`, `memo-key` or `defer` set); `callStep_noInl` (with a flag set the inline branch is not taken), `folStep_congr` (on such a call, one level depends only on calls of smaller weight that never inline: an inlineable formula has no `%2`, no `%9`, and its `%11` forms set a flag), `folLoopR_stable`, `folLoopR_at_weight`: on such a formula the result is the same at every fuel from `folW fol` on, crashes included, whatever `res` is.  An inlined formula is inlineable, so inlining goes one level deep.  The fuel of a formula that inlines is not bounded by its weight: `[9 2 [0 1]]` (weight `8`) against `&+[g 0]` analyses the battery `g` inline. |
| `Ska/DirectCall.lean` | The two sock facts under the finiteness of the formulas called directly: `callStep_res_cape` (a `%2` calls `res` only when the product sock of its formula part is `&+[fa fb]`, a cape exactly `&`; on any other sock `callStep` does not depend on `res`), with `knit_ne_yes` of `FolLoopFacts.lean` (`+knit:so` never makes the cape `&`), so a formula consed at run time is called indirectly.  The finiteness itself is stated in the header and not proved. |
| `Ska/GraphClose.lean` | `GraphClosed` proved: `skaCallgraph_closed` (the final graph holds the root and every callee of an entry is an entry, for a persistent memo with `MemoWF`, `MemoNorm` and `MemoNoCallees`).  The argument is the field `CInv.clo` of the convergence invariant (`ConvInv.lean`): under `MemoNoCallees` every state is `Closed` and `FinCalls` (a finalized entry calls only finalized identities).  A push and a memo hit put entries with no callees (`closed_put_new`, `gitMi_noCallees`), a run's end one whose callees are entries (`closed_put_callees`, with the new clause of `RunInv` kept by `inG_frame` and `AnsW.exists_entry`), `+pop` keeps both by its `hready`, and `+cull` by `cull_closed`, whose hypothesis comes from `FinCalls` and `RestBelow` (the members below the root call nothing above it), true at the push and kept through the run and the pass loop (`restBelow_keep`, `up_of_split`, `ConvPass.lean`; a hypothesis of `PassP2`, `cull_ok2`, `cullPass_ok2`).  Checked on the real compiler (2026-11-24), 26 pokes of `tools/hoon/closed.hoon`: the returned graphs had 1760 entries and 4052 callee edges, none missing; an instrumented `+cull` and `+pop` (`tools/hoon/closedsteps.py`) found, at 3680 culls, no edge into the component among 2,195,392 edges from outside it and no missing callee, and at 4472 pops no edge from a finalized entry into `order` among 2,354,100 and no missing callee. |
| `Ska/DatFacts.lean` | `NoAx0` (no `%10` at axis `0`); `folLoop_noAx0`: every nomm of `fol-loop` has it, at every fuel; `wf_inhab`: a well-formed sock that is not `%void` has a noun (its data); `patchProd_inhab`: `+patch-prod` keeps a noun.  With them the convergence invariant has `CInv.dat` (every graph entry is a memo hit that `order` does not hold, or has `NoAx0`, a code requirement with a noun and, once out of `order`, a product that is not `%void`), which gives `skaCallgraph_dat` and `memoHit_dat` (`ConvAna.lean`, under the memo invariant `MemoDat`), `PokeInv.dat` and `skaPoke_dat` (`PokeConv.lean`). |

| `Ska/PokeJets.lean` | Models of `+reverse-bell-graph`, `+hot-stale` and `+del-bells`. The stale set has its seeds and is closed under callers (`hotStale_closed`). What `CompHyp.jets` still needs is a locality property of the compile arms. |

Checked on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/optcheck.hoon`, 135 cases, raw code, every pass of every round and the output): no failure. |

| `Ska/OptDeadSim.lean` | **`dead_keeps`**, **`deadKeeps`**: `+remove-dead-code` on `(flop topo)` keeps the code (`Keeps`) on a `topo` with `TopoOK`.
