| `Ska/OptAliasSSADead.lean` | **`ssk_dead`** (the field `dead`, on `TopoOK`): `+remove-dead-code` keeps `AliasSSAB`.  `didx l S j` places the `j`-th op `deadF` keeps (`ssk_dd_head`, `ssk_dd_len`, `ssk_dd_mono`, `ssk_dd_lt`, `ssk_dd_mem`, `ssk_dd_sub`).  The live set of `deadF` only grows backwards (`ssk_dd_live`), a register read at a place is live there (`ssk_dd_read`), and along a path the live set at a block's start is in `Sf` of every earlier block (`DeadW.jmp`), so every op before a read that writes the read register is kept (`ssk_dd_def`).  `ssk_dead_walk` is `dead_walk` with the labels outside `topo` absent after. |
| `Ska/OptAliasSSADriver.lean` | `HopsSSA` (the field `hops` of `SSAKeepR`), `ssaKeepR_of` (the other three fields from `ssk_rub`, `ssk_mid`, `ssk_dead`) and **`optimize_keeps_ssaH`**: `optimizeF` keeps the code on `DInvS` given `DriverRestSSA` and `HopsSSA`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasSSAHops.lean` | **`ssk_hops_first`**: `+remove-hops` keeps `DefFirst`.  The moves of a merged hop define the next block's parameters and read the hop's arguments (`ssk_seg_defs`, `ssk_seg_ops`), `DefFirst` of a merged block over paths before by induction on `MTail` (`ssk_mt_first`), a path after lifts through the chains (`ssk_mt_path`), every definition of a merged block is one of a chain member (`ssk_mt_defs`).  `HopsOnce` (the pass gives `DefOnceB`) is what is left of `HopsSSA` (`hopsSSA_of`), and **`optimize_keeps_ssaO`** is the driver theorem with `DriverRestSSA` and `HopsOnce`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasFacts.lean` | `InfoHolds R i v` (clause 2 of `InfoOK`: the facts of `i` hold of the value `v`, `R` the values of the registers).  **`info_joinL`**, **`info_joinR`**: `joinReg` is weaker than each side.  **`info_param`**: `paramInfo` holds of the value an edge passed whenever that edge's argument facts held of it (`info_fold`, `info_clear`, `info_loob`). |
| `Ska/OptAliasSSAOnce.lean` | **`hk_once`**, **`hopsOnce_proved`**: `+remove-hops` keeps `DefOnceB` (on `Acyclic`, `ParNodup`, `TopoInv`), so `HopsOnce` holds and **`optimize_keeps_ssaD`** is the driver theorem with `DriverRestSSA` alone.  A block after is the merge of a chain (`HkMem`, `HkMerges`: each member hops to the next, whose only predecessor in `rev` it is).  `hk_sites` maps each place of a merged body to a definition before (an op of the member `x` at `j` to `(x, some j)`, a move into a parameter of `x` to `(x, none)`), injectively for a fixed register (`ParNodup` for the moves of one hop, the rank of `Acyclic` for distinct members).  A block reachable after is merged into no block reachable before (`hk_no_merged`: the block that jumps to it after ends its chain with `stop`), so the chains of two reachable blocks are disjoint (`hk_disj`), and a dead fill before is one after (`hk_live`: a `%bom` member ends its chain, and a read after is a read before in the chain). |
| `Ska/OptAliasSound.lean` | Step (b) of `+alias`.  **`as_op`**: one step of `aliasOp` keeps `AOK st R Done` (clauses 1 to 3 of `InfoOK`: the registers of `imml n` hold `n`, the facts of `loc` hold by `InfoHolds`, `old d` holds the value of a done `d`) for every op, with the destination added to `Done`, given `OpSem op R R2` (the op went on), its sources read correctly through `old` and not renamed to the destination, and a fresh destination (`AFresh`: in no `imml`, named by no fact, its own facts empty, not done, no done register renamed to it, not renamed itself).  Each dropped op is justified by the fact it reads (`imml` for `%imm`, `decOf` for `%inc`, `hedOf ∩ telOf` for `%con`, `hasHed`, `hasTel`, the source's `old` for `%mov`).  **`as_sem`**: an op of `execPole` that returns `%0` meets `OpSem` on the values `read`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasBody.lean` | The body fold of `+alias`.  **`ABInv st Def Done Cl`**, the structural side of the walk state: the registers of `imml`, the registers facts name and the keys with non-empty facts are in `Def` (defined so far on the path, with every register no definition writes), `old` maps `Done` into `Def`, a register outside `Def` in `Cl` keeps its own name.  **`ab_op`**: `aliasOp` keeps it when the op's sources are done and its destination is not in `Def`; `ab_fresh` and `ab_src` give `AFresh` and the source condition of `as_op` from it.  **`as_body`**: along a run of the ops of a body (`OpsRun`, one `OpSem` per op) that meets `BodyOK` (sources done, destinations not yet defined, op by op), `aliasBody` keeps `AOK` and `ABInv`; **`as_body_at`** gives both at every place of a body through `aliasBody_append`.  `AFresh` no longer asks that the destination keep its own name: `as_op` adds it to `Done` only when it does, since a dead fill walked earlier may rename a register that is never read. |
| `Ska/OptAliasBodySSA.lean` | **`bodyOK_ssa`**: on blocks with `AliasSSAB` and `Acyclic`, at the end `o` of a path from `0` (`BPath`), `BodyOK` holds of the body up to any place before which no op is a dead fill, with `Def` = `PathDef` (no definition writes it, a parameter of the block, or a definition of an earlier block of the path), `Cl` = `ClR` (no dead fill, or a reachable read) and any `Done` holding the registers of `PathDef` in `ClR` whose dead fills are all in `o`.  Sources come from `DefFirst` (`bodyOK_of` turns places into `BodyOK`), destinations are definitions that count, distinct by `DefOnceB` from every earlier definition on the path (`bdef_nodup`: an acyclic path repeats no block; `bdef_jumps`: an earlier block jumps, so holds no dead fill). |
| `Ska/OptAliasRev.lean` | **`AliasFieldSSAB` was false as stated**: `+alias` walks a block of `topo` that `rev` lists a predecessor for even when no jump reaches it, and its ops then rename registers in the global `old` (`tools/hoon/aliasrev.hoon`, on the real pass: `0w0` hops to `0w2`, the unreachable `0w1` is `[%mov 0v5 0v2]` `[%bom ~]`, `0w2` is `[%imm 5 0v2]` `[%don 0v2]`, and with `0w0` added to `rev-cfg` as a predecessor of `0w1` the end of `0w2` becomes `[%don 0v5]`, so `+eval-blob` returns `5` before and `7` after; `rev-cfg` alone drops `0w1`).  `TopoInv` allows that, so the field now also assumes **`RevSound`** (every block `rev` lists for `o` is a member of `topo` that jumps to `o`).  **`revCfg_sound`**: `+rev-cfg` gives it; `rvs_eq`, `rvs_rub` (`+remove-hops`, `+remove-useless-branching`, `+remove-empty-middle` without a change) and `rvs_shape` (`+trim-trace-hints`, `+remove-dead-code`) keep it, so the driver carries `TopoInvR` = `TopoInv` and `RevSound` (`driverHypS_of`), and `DriverRestSSA.alias` assumes it. |
| `Ska/OptAliasWalk.lean` | The blocks `+alias` walks are reachable.  **`ARev`** (what `rev.gen` lists after a prefix of `topo`: real predecessors in the blocks before, without duplicates, a walked one reachable) holds at the start (`arev_init`, from `RevSound`) and after each block (**`arev_step`**: a dropped block deletes all its edges, `awr_drop`; a walked one only deletes, `awr_fin`; a listed predecessor of the next block comes before it in `topo`, `awr_pre`, so the block is reachable when it is walked).  **`aliasWalk_reach`**: every block the walk puts in `new` is reachable in the blocks before. |
| `Ska/OptAliasStart.lean` | The facts at the start of a walked block (`FactsOK R m`: the facts of `m` hold on the register file `R`; `ImmsOK` for the immediates).  **`ast_start`**: the facts of `aliasStart` hold on the file at the end of the predecessor `q` the execution came from, when `q`'s end facts hold there and, if `q` is the only listed predecessor, `q`'s end in the code after goes to the block on that file (`GoesTo`, which justifies each refinement of a `%brn`, `%brz`, `%clq`); `ast_imms` likewise, `ast_entry` and `ast_imms_nil` at block `0`.  **`ast_pars`**: the parameter loop gives facts that hold on the file after the moves of the jump, along the edge taken, when no end fact of `q` names a parameter (`ast_param_names`: `param-info` names only what every edge's facts name; `ast_frame_set`). |
| `Ska/OptAliasFin.lean` | The end of a block of `+alias` (`aliasFin`, `aliasFold`).  `takenR`, `endR`: the jump an end takes and what an end that leaves the function returns, on the values (`afn_taken`, `afn_endOf`: `Path.taken` and `Path.endOf` at an end).  **`afn_jump`**: on a file where the facts at the end hold (`FactsOK`) and every register the end reads reads through `old` what it holds, the end after takes the jump the end before takes, to the same block, with the arguments renamed by `old`, and the edge taken stays listed in `rev` (a fold goes to the side the facts give: a `%clq` on a known cell or atom, an `%eqq` of two registers renamed to one, a `%brn` or `%brz` on a known immediate; a loobean `%brn` becomes a `%brz`).  **`afn_goesTo`**: such an end goes to its target in the sense of `GoesTo`.  **`afn_end`**: an end that leaves the function returns the same, given that the test of a `%brn` holds a value (a `%brn` on a known immediate that is no loobean becomes `[%bom ~]`).  `%lti` is excluded: `aliasFin` keeps it without renaming its register (see Gaps 12.8). |
| `Ska/OptAliasEdge.lean` | The start of a block along the edge taken.  **`aedge_aok`**: `AOK` at the end of the predecessor `q` the execution came from gives `AOK` at the start of `o` (the global `old`, the facts of `aliasStart` refined by `aliasPars`, the immediates of `aliasStartImms`) on the file after the pass, given the jump `q`'s end takes in the code after (`afn_jump`), the values of the parameters, that no fact or immediate at `q`'s end names a parameter of `o`, and that a register done at `o` is done at `q`'s end with the same `old` (or is a parameter keeping its name).  **`aedge_abinv`**: likewise `ABInv`, given `Def` at `q`'s end and the parameters inside `Def` at `o`, the registers `q`'s end reads in `Def` at `q`'s end, and the `self` clause of the global `old`.  Helpers: `ast_start_names` (the start names only what `q`'s facts name), `ast_start_keys` (a non-empty fact at the start is at a key where `q` has one, or at the register `q`'s end tests; `joinReg {} y = {}` and `joinReg x {} = {}`), `ast_imms_sub`, `ast_pars_names`, `aedge_mem` (the jump taken is an edge), `aedge_pass` (`+eval-pass` on distinct parameters that are no arguments). |
| `Ska/OptAliasFrame.lean` | What a step of the walk leaves alone: **`awk_op_old`**, **`awk_body_old`** (an op, a body changes `old` only at its destinations), **`awk_block`**, **`awk_walk`** (a block, a part of `topo` changes `old` only at the destinations of its ops, and `info`, `imms`, `new` only at its labels).  **`awk_fin_ssa`**: on `AliasSSAB`, at the end of a path from `0`, a register the end reads is in `PathDef` or written by an op of the block, in `ClR`, with every dead fill in the block (from `DefFirst` at the place `|body|`), what `afn_jump` needs of `Done` at the end (`awk_finReads_sub`). |
| `Ska/OptAliasLti.lean` | **`NoLti`** (no end is `%lti`, which `aliasFin` keeps without renaming its register): the driver carries it in `DInvS` (now `DInvT`, `AliasSSAB`, `NoLti`), so the raw blocks owe it through `rawDInv`, `AliasFieldSSAB` assumes and keeps it, and every pass of the loop keeps it: **`nolti_shape`** (`+trim-trace-hints`, `+remove-dead-code` keep every end), **`nolti_rub`** (a two-way end becomes a hop or stays), **`nolti_mid`** (a retargeted end keeps its tag), **`nolti_hops`** (a merged block ends as a block before, `MTail.fin_of`).  `driverHypS_of` and the driver theorems (`optimize_keeps_ssaD`) keep their statements. |
| `Ska/OptAliasInv.lean` | Pieces of the walk invariant of `+alias` over executions.  **`DefW bl pre`** (`Def` independent of the path: no block defines it, or a `LiveDef` in a block of the walked prefix `pre`); `awi_bodyOK_mono` (`BodyOK` for a larger `Done` and a `Def` adding no destination), **`awi_par_notDefW`**, **`awi_dst_notDefW`** (on `DefOnceB`, a parameter, and a destination before the first dead fill, of a reachable block outside `pre` are outside `DefW`), **`awi_bodyOK`** (`bodyOK_ssa` with `Def` = `DefW` and the parameters).  The global `old`: **`OldW`** (it renames only destinations of ops of walked, reachable blocks; `awi_old_walk`), **`awi_self`** (a `ClR` register outside `DefW` keeps its name, through `DefFirst` before a dead fill's read and `awi_path_pre`: a path to a walked block runs through walked blocks).  **`DoneE`** at a block end (on the path or by the block, `ClR`, no dead fill in `pre`), **`awi_frame_walk`**, **`awi_done_stable`**, **`awi_end_later`** (`AOK` and `ABInv` at the end of a walked jumping block survive the rest of the walk), **`DoneS`** at a block start, **`awi_fin_done`** (what an end reads is in `DoneE`, the `hold` of `afn_jump`), **`awi_edge_side`** (`hnm`, `hnmi`, `hDo` and `hself` of `aedge_aok`, `aedge_abinv`).  **`ACfgAt`**, **`awi_step_at`**, **`awi_entry_at`**: a configuration reached from the entry runs a suffix of the block at its label at the end of a `BPath`, which a jump step extends. |
| `Ska/OptAliasAll.lean` | The walk invariant of `+alias` over executions (README Gaps 12.8).  `epath es`: the path of blocks an execution with edges `es` took (`awa_entry_at`: `ACfgAt` along it).  `awa_steps_last`: an execution from the entry stays in block `0` or ends with a jump into its block, after which `awa_inblock_passed` gives the pass and the run of a prefix of the ops (`OpsRun`, through `as_sem`).  **`EndOK`**: at the end of a walked jumping block, the facts, immediates and block after are stored, the registers the end after reads are in `DefW`, `AOK` and `ABInv` hold with the global `old` and `DoneE` along the path taken, and the jump the end before takes is listed in `rev` and taken by the end after to the same block with its arguments renamed by the global `old` (so of the same values, `argV`).  `awa_start_entry`, `awa_start_jump`: `AOK` and `ABInv` at a block start from `entryInfo` or from `EndOK` at the predecessor taken (`aedge_aok`, `aedge_abinv`, `awi_edge_side`, `aedge_pass`); `awa_end`: `EndOK` at the end of the block just walked (`awi_bodyOK`, `as_body`, `afn_jump`, `awa_fin_reads`); `awa_end_step`, `awa_rebase`: one more block of the walk for an end walked before (`awi_end_later` for one block of a walk over the whole `topo`); `awa_rev_keep`: a block of the walk deletes from `rev` only itself.  **`AWInv`** (`ARev`, `OldW`, the edges of blocks not walked still listed, `EndOK` for every execution), `awa_start` (the start of the block just walked, for an execution standing in it), `awa_step`, `awa_walk`, `awa_walk_part`, **`alias_walk_endOK`** and **`alias_walk_inv`**.  **`alias_walk_at`**: at every configuration of an execution that passed the parameters of its block and runs its body from place `k`, the block after is the parameters, the ops kept from `aliasBody` split at `k` (state `sta` there) and the end of `aliasFin`, and when no op before `k` is a dead fill the facts and immediates of `sta` hold and every register the next op (or the end) reads reads through the `old` of `sta` the value it holds (`awa_read_done`, from `DefFirst`).  **`alias_walk_end`**: at the end of a block that leaves the function, the end after returns what the end before returns on the values of the code before (`afn_end`; a `%bom` end is kept as it is).  A first piece of the agreement relation: **`AKept`** (the values of the code after equal those of the code before at every register of an immediate, every register a fact names, and the `old` of every done register) and **`aedge_kept`**, its transfer along the edge taken, the jump after passing the arguments before renamed by `old`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasSim.lean` | Toward the simulation of `+alias`: the agreement relation `AKept` through the body.  `ACov` (the registers `AKept` covers), **`acov_op`** (after one step of `aliasOp` a covered register was covered before or is the destination of a kept op), **`akept_op`** (`AKept` after one step, the code after writing a kept destination with the same value and leaving a dropped one alone), **`akx_exec`** (a kept op after, with its reads renamed by `old`, runs as the op before when its reads read through `old` the values before: `XRel`, both crash or the same tone, trace, state and write), **`akd_exec`** (a dropped op before goes on, from `ReadsOK`, `LocalOK` and the facts), **`akb_op`** (one op forward on the values, with `AKept` after). |
| `Ska/OptAliasKeeps.lean` | Pieces of `KeepsB` for the model of `+alias` (README Gaps 12.8).  `aStart`: the state at the start of a body in the walk (`awa_start` now returns it).  **`alias_at_of`**: given the walk up to block `o` (`pre`, `g1`, `aStart`) and the state `sta` after the first `k` ops, at every configuration of an execution from the entry that passed the parameters of `o` and runs its body from `k`, every register read at `k` is done, and before the first dead fill `AOK` and `ABInv` hold at `sta` with `Done` the `bodyDone` of the first `k` ops from `DoneS`, and `BodyOK` holds for the next ops up to the first dead fill.  **`akept_jump`**: `AKept` at the end of the body of a walked jumping block, with the walk-time state there, rebased to the `old` of the walk at a later point and `DoneE` there (`awa_frame_seg`, `awa_done_seg`: the frame of the walk over a part of `topo`).  **`APres`** (presence: a done register present in the file before has its `old` present in the file after), **`apres_op`** (kept through one op, kept or dropped; a dropped `%mov` from a poisoned register copies the poison, `dropped_dst_val`), **`apass_loop`** (the pass after of the renamed arguments goes on when presence holds and neither an argument nor its renaming is a parameter), **`akp_edge`** (along the edge taken, the pass after goes on and `AKept` and presence hold at the start of the block jumped to), **`akb_live`** (one op of a live body forward with `AOK`, `ABInv`, `AKept` and presence after) and **`akj_jump`** (the jump at the end of a walked block with its pass: the end after on the file after takes the jump renamed, the pass after goes on, and `AKept` and presence hold at the start state of the block jumped to). |
| `Ska/OptAliasPick.lean` | The choice of `+alias` instantiated: `pickR`, the first key of `rap` of the `+silt` of a list of registers (on the treaps of the Hoon the root `n`, since `+rep` visits `n` first), and **`pickR_mem`**: it picks a member of a non-empty list (the hypothesis `hpick` of the model).  The pill link owes that the Hoon's `n` is this key. |
| `Ska/OptBranch.lean` | `+remove-useless-branching` (`rubFin`, `removeUselessBranching`) and `rub_keepsR`: it keeps the code (`KeepsR`; `rub_keeps` of `OptArgs.lean` gives `Keeps`), with no hypothesis on the CFG.  A simulation on paths (`RubRel`, `rub_fwd`, `rub_bwd`, `rub_bwd_end`, `rub_reads`); `ReadsOK` is used where a `%clq` or `%brz` became a hop and so no longer reads its register. |
| `Ska/OptHops.lean`, `Ska/OptMiddle.lean`, `Ska/OptDead.lean` | Models of `+remove-hops` (`hopSeg`, `hopChain`, `hopsStep`, `removeHops`), `+remove-empty-middle` (`rewriteJump`, `rewriteHop`, `mkSub`, `passArg`, `readersOf`, `removeEmptyMiddle`) and `+remove-dead-code` (`safeAssign`, `deadBody`, `removeDeadCode`), with their preservation statements as definitions (`HopsKeeps`, with `ParNodup` and `PassOK`, proved in `OptArgs.lean` from `OptHopsSim.lean`; `MiddleKeeps`, with `PassOK` of the input and of the result and `ArgsOK` on the entry, proved in `OptMiddleSim.lean`; `DeadKeeps`, `Keeps` on a `topo` with `TopoOK`, proved in `OptDeadSim.lean`). |
| `Ska/OptRegLe.lean` | `RegLe R R'`: at every register the same unit, or poison in `R` and a value in `R'`.  An op, a pass, a branch and an end whose real reads read values in `R` do the same on `R'` (`execPole_le`, `evalPass_le`, `taken_le`, `endOf_le`), and a step of the code before that does not crash read values (`execPole_reads`, `taken_reads`, `endOf_reads`). |
| `Ska/OptHopsPass.lean` | The moves of a merged hop (`hopSeg_eq`: `[%mov a p]` or `[%imm 0 p]` per argument, in the reverse order) against the pass of the code before: both are runs of pairs (`runPairs`, `evalPassLoop_pairs`, `runMoves_pairs`), whose order does not matter when the parameters are distinct and no argument is one (`runPairs_spec`); `passRel_init`: the registers after the moves are above those after the pass, and the two succeed together. |
| `Ska/OptHopsBlocks.lean` | The blocks after `+remove-hops`: `MTail` (the tail and end a chain of merges appends to a block), `hopChain_spec`, `hopsFold_spec`, `hops_blocks` (every block after is the merge of the block at its label, every label of `topo` holds one or was merged into its only predecessor in `rev`, block `0` holds the merge of block `0`), `revCfg_mem` (`+rev-cfg` lists every member of `topo` that jumps to a block). |
| `Ska/OptHopsSim.lean` | **`hops_keepsR_of`**: `+remove-hops` keeps the code (`KeepsR`) given `HopsHyp` (every reachable block in `topo`, `rev` listing the predecessors in `topo`, `ParNodup`, `PassOK`), `topo` without duplicates and `0` first (`hops_keeps_of`, `hops_keeps`, `hopsKeeps` of `OptArgs.lean` give `Keeps`); `hops_parNodup`, `hops_passOK`: the pass keeps both hypotheses.  A simulation on paths (`HopI`: the same position with registers in `RegLe`; `HopII`: a pass pending before, its moves running after), forward (`hop_fwd_step`, `hop_fwd_end`) without hypothesis on the reads, backward through a normal form (`hop_norm`, `hop_bwd_step`, `hop_bwd_end`, `hop_bwd_reads`) with `ReadsOK`. |
| `Ska/OptDriver.lean`, `Ska/AuditOpt.lean` | `+optimize-once` (`structLoop`, `optimizeOnce`) and `+optimize` (`optLoop`, `optimizeF`) over the pass models, with `+alias`, `+trim-trace-hints`, `+switch-chains` as parameters (`Passes`); `optimize_keeps`: `optimizeF` keeps the code on blocks with `BInv` given `DriverHyp` (each pass keeps the code at the arguments the driver gives, over two invariants: `BInv` of the blocks alone, which every pass keeps whatever its change flag, and `TInv` of the blocks, `topo` and `rev`, which `+bb-topo` with `+rev-cfg` makes and a structural pass keeps when it reports no change); the fields are discharged in `OptDriverFields.lean` and `OptDriverTopo.lean`.  Termination is `OptHalts`.  The axiom audit of these theorems. |
| `Ska/OptMiddleBlocks.lean` | The blocks after `+remove-empty-middle` (`midStep`, `mid_blocks`, `MidBlocks.back`: same labels, parameters and bodies, the end from `middleFin`); the pass keeps `ParNodup` (`mid_parNodup`), and keeps `PassOK` exactly under its hypothesis, which is `PassOK` of its result (`mid_passOK`); `+remove-useless-branching` keeps both with no hypothesis (`rub_parNodup`, `rub_passOK`). |
| `Ska/OptDriverFields.lean` | The invariants meant for `DriverHyp`: `BlkInv` (`ParNodup` and `PassOK`) and `TopoInv` (`TopoOK`, and `rev` lists every member of `topo` that jumps to a block; `topoInv_revCfg` from `TopoOK`).  Discharged: the semantic half and `BlkInv` of the fields `hops` (`hops_field`) and `rub` (`rub_field`, `rub_keepsB`), and of `middle` under its hypothesis `PassOK` of the result (`middle_blkInv`, `middle_field`, from `middle_keeps`); the `TopoInv` halves and `Acyclic` are in `OptDriverTopo.lean`. |
| `Ska/OptMiddleAgr.lean` | Register files that agree on a set (`Agr`): an op whose registers (`poleRegs`) lie in the set does the same on both and keeps the agreement (`execPole_agr`); two passes whose arguments pass the same units (`argU`) and pass the check of `+eval-pass` succeed together and agree on the parameters (`passLoop_agr`); `pass_sub`, the substitution of `rewrite-hop` (after a pass a parameter holds what the argument `mkSub` maps it to passes). |
| `Ska/OptMiddleWalk.lean` | The parameters of bypassable blocks (`Byp`), which only their own block mentions (`byp_owner`, from `readersOf`); the walks of the code before through the bypassed blocks where the code after takes a rewritten jump (`jump_walk` for a branch, registers unchanged; `hop_walk` for a hop, registers changed only at bypassable parameters, pending arguments passing the units of the substituted ones); `taken_mid` (the jump after against the jump before). |
| `Ska/OptMiddleSim.lean` | **`middle_keeps_of`**, **`middleKeeps`**: `+remove-empty-middle` keeps `execEntry`, `ReadsOK` and `ArgsOK` on every entry with `ArgsOK`, given `PassOK` of the input and of the result.  A simulation on paths with one relation (`MidI`: the same position, pending arguments passing the same units, registers agreeing off `Byp` and on what the block mentions once its pass is done), matched step for step in both directions after the walks (`mid_fwd_step`, `mid_bwd_step`), the same ends (`mid_endOf`) and reads (`mid_reads`).  `ReadsOK` is not needed; `ArgsOK` is, with the witness in the header (a hop `[0v5]` into an empty block whose parameter nothing mentions, `0v5` not in the file: the code before crashes at the pass, the code after goes on). |
| `Ska/OptArgs.lean` | `ArgsOK` through the other proved passes, given `ReadsOK`: `rub_argsOK` (`+remove-useless-branching`, same arguments and registers), `hops_argsOK_of` (`+remove-hops`, through `RegLe.get_none`); `LocalOK` through them: `rub_localOK` (an op after is the op before at the same place or an epilogue `%equ`/`%lob`), `hops_localOK_of` (the same op on registers above, or a merged `%mov` on the way to a pass that succeeds before: `ArgsOK`, the check and the counts, which `HopII` now records), `middle_localOK` (the same op on registers that agree on what it mentions); with them **`Keeps`** of the three proved passes: `rub_keeps` (no hypothesis), `hops_keeps_of`, `hops_keeps`, `hopsKeeps` (the hypotheses of `hops_keepsR_of`), `middle_keeps` (`PassOK` of the input and of the result). |
| `Ska/PokeModel.lean` | A model of `+poke` (`+compiled-mono`, `+compiled-nary`, the `%full` and `%dire` branches; the compile arms, `%jets` and `%fast` are parameters). `pokeS_sound`, `pokeS_invKept`, `invS_init` from two named hypotheses: `CompHyp` (what `+comp`, `+optimize` and the cache must supply) and `AnaHyp`. `gap_of` gives the shapes of `Gap.contract`, `Gap.invKept` and `Gap.invStart` of `lemmas/Assembly.lean`. |
| `Ska/BugWitness.lean` | The three places where the Hoon broke the contract when this was written (an inlined call dropping its formula's formula, malformed operands, `%bare`). Fixed in the Hoon since; the models still describe the old code. |
| `NockSmallStep.lean`, `Nomm.lean`, `DecExample.lean`, `NOMM_PROOF.md` | The earlier work: small-step semantics, a model of `nomm-1`, a toy decrement. |

Every main theorem depends on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`
only.  There is no `sorry` and no `native_decide`.

## How a proof about an arm goes

A proof never unfolds Nock by hand.  It opens the arm's formula once
(`nock_open dec.loop`), and `simp only [nock_exec, ...]` runs it on a subject
that has variables in it, up to the first branch that the hypotheses do not
decide.  Calls into other arms are rewritten by those arms' theorems, and
recursion by the induction hypothesis.  For example the whole proof that the
Nock of `+put:by` computes `putF` is nine lines for ten cases.

Formulas that are constants stay closed until a proof opens them, so a
recursive call cannot unfold forever.

A theorem about a gate quantifies over the gate's context `c` and assumes
only what the gate's Nock reads from it: a predicate such as `LthIn c` says
that `c` holds the arm `+lth` at `lth.axis` and what `+lth` reads in turn.
The predicates are `abbrev`s of conjunctions of `slot?` equations, so a
hypothesis `hc : LthIn c` given to `simp` rewrites the `slot?` that the
executor cannot compute on the variable `c`.  Each family file proves that
the pill's layer satisfies its predicates (`arith_layer1`,
`mugNeeds_layer2`), so a change elsewhere in the pill leaves the theorems
standing.

Arithmetic in models stays out of reach of reduction.  The models of `+muk`
and `+mug` recurse through generic combinators (`iterDown`, `retry`,
`foldNoun`), and they are tied to the expressions that the execution
produces by small equations over variables (`mukBlock_eq`, `fmix32_eq`),
proved by `delta` and `Eq.refl`.  An equation lemma of a recursive
definition whose body calls the murmur3 arithmetic, or an `rfl` between two
such expressions that are not syntactically equal, took the elaborator past
3 GB.

A function that `simp` runs on the pill's formulas must not be structurally
recursive.  `nockW` of `Iter.lean` was first defined by structural recursion
on the formula, and `simp only` with its rules on the loop of `+mink` took
more than 2 GB after a few hundred steps, where the same rules for `nock` ran
in half a second.  Defined with `partial_fixpoint`, like `nock`, it runs as
fast as `nock`.

## Regenerating from a new pill

```sh
python3 tools/pill2core.py pill/steel.pill core/Pill.lean
python3 tools/pill2lean.py pill/steel.pill lemmas/Pill
python3 tools/pill2cue.py pill/steel.pill lemmas/Pill/Cue   # after pill2lean
```

`pill2cue.py` writes only the files whose text changes.  Its files hold no
terms: the windows and the nouns are defined by the commands of
`CueTac.lean` from the bits of `pill`, and the theorems are one line each.

`pill/steel.pill` is the steel pill of this branch (sha256 `76ea362e...`,
793,137 bytes, built from the Hoon at 1813f09): its
`desk/sys/hoon.hoon` built from nothing and `desk/lib/nock-compilation.hoon`
on it, by the vere test harness without a ship (`U3NC_STEEL_GEN=out.pill`,
`U3NC_STEEL_SYS`, `U3NC_STEEL_LIB`).  `pill2lean.py` finds the arms by their
`%fast` labels, and the few arms without one through the arms that call them,
so a new pill needs no edits to the proofs unless the Nock of an arm changed.
The axes in `core/Statement.lean` are not regenerated: check them after a
change to the arms of the last three cores of the library.

## Caching

The generated files and the theorem files are Lean modules.  An arm file
exports its nouns without their bodies, except the exposed axis constants.
The arm's own theorem file reads the bodies with `import all`, and its callers
import only its theorems.  So:

1. A change to the Nock of one arm, after regeneration, changes its arm file
   only: an arm file is byte-identical when the arm's Nock and axis did not
   change, and the chain refers to arms by name.  It rechecks the arm file,
   the arm's theorem file, `Facts.lean` and `Audit.lean`, and nothing else,
   as long as the arm's theorems still hold.  (Measured by changing a
   character of the tape in `dec.battery`.)
2. A change to a declaration of the chain rebuilds the rest of the chain,
   `Facts.lean` and the layer files, and no gate theorem file.  A comment
   rebuilds the edited file only.
3. A clean build takes about 100 seconds (6 min 50 s of CPU) and peaks at
   2.7 GB for the whole build (measured 2026-11-23 under a 3 GB scope; before
   `lemmas/Pill/Cue/` it was 100 s, 5 min 35 s and 2.6 GB).  The files of
   `Pill/Cue/` form chains (`Win`, `J000`, ..., `KV`, then the `D` files in
   order), so that at most one of them is in memory at a time: with the `D`
   files built in parallel the build went past 3 GB.  The `D` files set
   `Elab.async false`, which keeps a theorem's elaboration from living until
   the end of the file (1 GB instead of 0.3 GB for 24,000 tokens).

## Gaps

1. **The statements are proved from a list of hypotheses.**
   `Assembly.lean` proves `Full` from `Gap`; what is left of the project is
   that list.
   One of them, `Halting`, was false of the Hoon up to 1813f09: the pass
   loop of `+analyze` did not end on the poke of `tools/hoon/swaploop.hoon`
   (`Ska/PassLoop.lean`).  The pill of this branch is built from the Hoon at
   1813f09, where `+run` unites `less-code` with the old entry's cape, and
   that poke returns.  `Halting` is not proved: `CgHalts` would need the
   measure of the header of `Ska/PassLoop.lean` (per entry, the cape size of
   `less-code`, which now only grows inside the cape of `bus`, then the
   knowledge of the product, which only shrinks, then `indi` and `seek`),
   with the provenance `map` and a culled member found again left open.
   Four changes of the Hoon made this morning are on the pill and in the
   models: the guard of `+bake-graph` (8f2162e, `Bake.bakeOf`,
   `Ska/VariantCollision.lean`), the open trace hints counted per key in
   `+trim-trace-hints` (753abe9, `Ska/OptTrim.lean`), the memo lookup of the
   `resolve` loop at the current identity's sock (1df76d0,
   `Ana.resolveStep`, linked by `resolveStepH_eq` and `resolve_stepW`), and
   the union of `less-code` with the old cape (1813f09, `runDatum`, linked
   by `run_datum_new` and `run_stepW`).  The links of the analysis
   (`resolve_iff`, `fol_loop_iff`, `analyze_iff`, `ska_callgraph_iff`,
   `ska_poke_ana_iff_pill`, `ska_poke_iff`) and its model theorems
   (`skaCallgraph_frozen`, `skaPoke_sound`, `skaPokeB_sound`,
   `skaCallgraph_closed`, the fuel monotonicity of `AnaFuel.lean`,
   `resolve_he_bound`) hold of the fixed compiler with their statements.
2. **The pill is tied to `root`** (`cueRoot` of `Assembly.lean`, from
   `cue_pill` of `lemmas/Pill/Cue/Root.lean`).  The kernel cannot evaluate
   `cue` on 793 KB in one piece, so `CueRel.lean` proves once that `cue`
   returns what the relation `Dec` accepts, and 381 generated theorems check
   the 546,193 tokens of the jam in pieces of at most 2000, reading the bits
   through windows of two chunks.  The table of back-references is a fixed
   list of 23,358 entries, numbered in the order in which their decoding ends;
   a derivation counts the entries decoded so far, so nothing is checked about
   the bits that no reference points to.  The noun of the derivation is
   compared with `root` by the kernel (`s0_eq`), in under a second.
3. **`IsMap` and `+apt:by` differ on one shape.**  `apt_by` proves that the
   pill's `+apt:by` accepts exactly the `IsMap` nouns and the lone nodes
   `[x ~ ~]` with an atom `x`: on a node without bounds and without children
   `+apt:by` never reads its head.
4. **Failure is proved for few arms.**  `(dec 0)`, `+sub` below zero,
   `+dvr`, `+div`, `+mod` by zero and `+snag` past the end crash; nothing is
   proved about gates slammed on nouns of the wrong shape, nor about nouns on
   which a model is `none`.
5. **Not done among the arms of the library:** `+uni:by`, `+int` of `+by` and
   `+in`, and `+dif` of `+by` (`+dif:in` is done, `Hoon/DifIn.lean`, and
   `+uni:in`, `Hoon/UniIn.lean`).
6. **The arms of `+ca` and `+so` are all done.**  No arm of `+ca` or `+so`
   disagrees with B's model.
7. **The virtualization arms are done** up to formatting: `+mink`, `+monk`,
   `+mack`, `+mook` and `+mock`.  `+scow`, `+smyt`, `+crip` and `+soft` are
   not proved; `mock_pill_iff` assumes what they compute, field by field of
   `Lib`.
8. **The small arms of the analysis are done:** `+inlineable`, `+safe-nomm`,
   `+safe-fol-fol`, `+knit-anno`, `+patch-prod`, `+msg-sock`, `+msg-anno`
   agree with their models on every input.
9. **The interpreter is done in part.**  `+read`, `+store`, `+move`,
   `+eval-pass` and the ops of `+eval-pole` that stay in the function agree
   with `Ska/Interp.lean` on maps of `TreapMap mugF`, and so do the ops that
   leave it (`Compiler/EvalCall.lean`), given the poke and what `+eval-top`
   and `+eval-blob` compute, and what `EvalTop` says, the pill's `+eval-top`
   returns (`eval_top_of_model`) and conversely (`eval_top_iff`), for a poke
   that a model `pk` computes and that keeps layers 2 and 4 (`PokeIs`,
   `CorIn`); `+eval-rend` and `+eval-mook` likewise (`Compiler/EvalRend.lean`).
   Not done: that `+poke` computes `pk` (`PokeModel` of `Assembly.lean`); that
   it keeps the layers is proved (`pokeFrame_corIn`).  The arms that thread the compiler's core read their context
   through its tail `cor`, so after a poke they read the new `cor`: a theorem
   about them needs the poke to keep layer 2 at `read.layer2.axis`.

Two facts from the raw blocks fix how the proxy cases must be stated.  First, `+to-straight` replaces each proxy register by its definition (`unproxy`), so the proxy itself is never written in the Gen blocks, and a run that makes proxies is compared with the Gen blocks after `unproxy` of the final `cond`.  Done for this (`CompUnproxy.lean`): `AgreeU` (`AgreeP` against the unproxied blocks, equal to `AgreeP` with no proxy, `agreeU_empty`), `CondFresh` (the relaxed `cnd`: `cond` changes only at registers allocated in the run, met by `+proxy`), and the commutation of `+chase` and of the renaming path with unproxying (`chase_ren`, `renameBlocks_ren`).  Done for the top: the variant of `toStraight_entryR` for `toStraightF` with proxies (`toStraightF_entryU`, `CompUnproxyEntry.lean`), which compares the code with the unproxied program and so does not itself need the inputs to be outside the keys of `cond`.  Done for the top (`CompUnproxyFill.lean`, `CompUnproxyTop.lean`, 2026-11-24): `RunOKU` (`RunOKP` with `CondFresh g0 g1` in place of `cnd`, `AgreeU` by the run's final `cond` in `sem`, and fact (ii) below as the fields `ncond` and `econd`), `finishLF_compDoneU` and `finishLF_bellContractU` in the shape of the `R` versions, and `RunOKP.toU`, through which the `X` and `71` theorems are restated (`finishLF_bellContractXU`, `finishLF_bellContract71U`).  The fill ops of the final pass mention only the input registers, the lazy need taken there and fresh registers (`coerceLazyL_regs`), the input and fresh registers lie above every key of `cond` (`CondBelow`) and the lazy needs' registers are no keys (`ncond`, `econd`), so unproxying leaves the fill ops unchanged (`unproxy_ops`); that gives the lazy-block clause of `AgreeU` and `hag` of `toStraightF_entryU`, with the program `c` built as the Gen blocks unproxied (`mapVals`) and the entry block without its fill ops.  So the top theorems now ask only `RunOKU` of the run; no relation proves `RunOKU` of a run that makes a proxy yet, and the formulas covered are those of the `R` theorems.  `RunOKU.sem` takes `AgreeU` by the run's own final `cond`; to compose runs, as the collapse case needs, it must take a later `cond` that agrees with it below `g1.re`, which needs the invariant that the blocks a run emits or changes mention no register at or above its end. The leaf conversion from `RunOK` with the relaxed hypothesis is the hard part, because `RunOK.sem` gives only some register file `R` at the goal's jump (`goJ regs je = goJ R z`) whose `Keep` frame says nothing of the run's own sure need, and `goJ` is an equation of `Option`s, so a path argument on the real program `c` alone does not reach `z`: the run might end inside the code with the same value, or diverge.  The route (`CompLeafKeep.lean`, pieces done 2026-11-24): add `Clear g0` to the hypotheses of the leaf (it holds of `gen0` and every `GrowU` keeps it, so `OKP` and the other inductions can carry it), so that every block of `g1` from `g0.bo` on is the run's (`clear_newU`), writes only the footprint `Q` of `run_foot` and jumps below its label (`run_down`); apply `RunOK.sem` to the program `regionProg c g1.blocks g0.bo g1.bo z.there bz` (it agrees with `g1` on the run's labels) with `bz = ⟨[], [imm (A 0) k], don k⟩`, which returns, so by `run_iff_path` and `steps_exit` the path from `je` either ends inside the run's labels (excluded: by `steps_transfer` the same path ends the same way under `bz = ⟨[], [], bom ~⟩`, where `RunOK` says the run returns `%2`) or leaves them first at a jump `j` whose label has a block, which is only `z.there`, with `j.args = []` and the registers outside `Q` those of `regs`; transfer that prefix to `c` (`steps_transfer`, `steps_run`, `run_jump`), so `goJ c regs je = goJ c R' z` for the configuration's `R'`, whose state and trace are those of the start by the two programs; then `R'` keeps every register outside `Q`, and the registers of `ned` are outside `Q` when they are at or above `g1.re` or in the sure need.  For a goal `%next` the same, with one program `bz = ⟨[], [], don r⟩` for each register `r` of the goal's need to get `HoldsN` of `R'` (the `R` of `RunOK` holds it in each program). The relations themselves keep their strict `sem`: the leaf conversion (`CompLeafKeep.lean`) and the footprints (`CompFootX.lean`) import the files that define and build them, so their proofs cannot use those; the relaxed `sem` is proved beside them by the same induction (`runP_semR` on `FragP`, `runN_semR` on `FragN`, every `%6` included), and the top theorems are unchanged. Done since (`CompCollapseRun.lean`, 2026-11-24): the run lemma and the composition for the sure need `this d`.  `collRun_n4` gives `CollRunU` of `[4 p]` under `%next laz th` with entries (`laz.sure.ned = this d`, looks at axis `1`): the walk's ops at each consumed entry with `CollP d` at the run's final `cond`, `ProxyFor d` in place of `cnd`, facts (ii) and (i5) proved from the model (the keys of the final `cond` are those of the start, below its `re`, and the proxies, below the walk's end, while the result's registers are `arg` or later), and the relaxed `sem` against the blocks unproxied by any `cond` that agrees with the run's below its end.  The `sem` needs that the blocks of `p` mention no proxy, which nothing proved: `BelowIn` bounds the registers of a run's blocks from above only.  So `collRun_n4` takes `AboveX p` (the blocks of a run of `p` for the goal `this a` mention only registers from `a` on), proved for `[0 axe]` with `2 ≤ axe` (`aboveX_n0`, no block; `[0 0]` emits a `%bom` block, which needs `Clear` of the start to be seen as the only block at its label).  `runOKU_seven4` composes it with `q` in `FragX` for `%done` from `gen0`: `q`'s `SemGR` at `pair ned (this d)` holding `[s0 v]` (`InR` admits `d` as the sure need of `q`'s result), `q`'s blocks unproxied unchanged (they mention registers below `q`'s end, where the final `cond` is empty), and `q`'s lazy blocks filled by the unproxied walk ops through `collP_fillSem`.  `finishLF_bellContract74U` is the top theorem for `[7 [4 p] q]` with `p`, `q` in `FragX`, `AboveX p`, at most 255 `%6`, and the hypothesis `hsq` that the run of `q` for `%done` from `gen0` returns the sure need `this d` with looks at axis `1`.  The target `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` meets every hypothesis on the formula (an `example` there); `hsq` of its `q` is not proved, since it needs the run of the `%6` under `%done` computed through `+copy-lazy`.  Left for the collapse case, in this order: `hsq` for a class of `q` (or `collRun_n4` for the other three shapes of `+sure-require-look` of the sure need: `none` takes a fresh `r`, `pair` and `both` emit a `%bom` block and take its label as `there`, and the gate then puts ops at `there` too); `AboveX` over `FragX` (an induction like `runX_keeps` with the lower bound); `[3 p]` (the same walk, then `+forl` and `%clq`); `[7 [4 p] q]` under goals other than `%done` (through `SemGR` in place of `RunOKU`).  The plan before this, which changed the structures themselves (kept for the record): they cannot change one at a time, because `RunOKG.toP` turns `RunOKG` into `RunOKP` and `RunOKG.toN` into `RunOKN` by passing the strict hypothesis on, and the `%4`, `%3`, `%7`, cons and `%5` cases of `CompFragM.lean`, `CompBinaryN.lean`, `CompSevenN.lean`, `CompN10N.lean`, `CompConsN.lean` reach `RunOKP` through `RunOKG`; so `RunOKP`, `RunOKG`, `RunOKN` (and then `RunOKE`) change together, each proof of a `sem` that passes the input registers across code it emits needing `FootR.wr` with `steps_frame` in place of the bound `g1.re ≤ r`, and `Clear` and `LeafJ` threaded like `UpBelow` (`OKP` and `NextOK` take them; `runX_ok` already restricts `%pick` and `%next` to jumps without arguments).  The relations, as planned before: the relaxed hypothesis `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` in `RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem` and `RunOKE.sem` needs `runX_foot` and, in each proof of a `sem` that passes the input registers across emitted code, `FootR.wr` with `steps_frame`.  The semantic frame lemma is done (`CompFrame.lean`, 2026-11-24): `step_frame`, `steps_frame`, `run_frame`, `execEntry_frame`, `steps_reads_frame` (a path through blocks that write only `Q` keeps every register outside `Q`, poison included).  The relaxed `sem` cannot be derived from the present `sem` with it, beside the structure.  The reason is the clause `FillSem` of `AgreeP`: it quantifies over every register file `R` that holds `ned` for `s0`.  The present `sem` accepts only an `AgreeP` whose `ned` lies at or above `g1.re`, and when `ned` overlaps the sure need at a register `d` below `g1.re`, the fill ops `A` read `d` (in the collapse case they are the `%mov d r'`), so no `ned` above `g1.re` makes `FillSem` of those `A` hold: `R` says nothing about `d`.  Changing the program instead (copying `d` to a fresh register and renaming the fill ops) needs a simulation of whole runs between two programs, and it fails because `GoalSem` includes everything after the goal's jump, where an arbitrary program may write `d` and jump back into the lazy blocks.  So the relaxed hypothesis must go into the structures (`RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem`, `RunOKE.sem`) and each proof of a `sem` must be changed where it shows that the input registers are kept across code it emits: the old argument (the written register is below `g1.re`, the input registers at or above it) becomes the written register is below `g1.re` and not in the result's sure need, which is `Foot.wr` (and, for the lazy blocks reached, `steps_frame` with `Q` the footprint).  That needs `Foot` for every case the relations cover, so `%10` and `FragX` come first.  Not yet: (i5) for runs that make proxies (`+kern`, `+collapse-lazy-atom` under a goal with entries), where `cond` changes and `+sect` threads proxies, so `KeepR.cnd` must give way to `CondFresh` and the definitions in `cond` must be shown below `re`.  Tested on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/selfkeep.py`, check i5, 2026-11-24): every register a new or changed block of a run mentions (parameters, ops, the terminator with its jump arguments) is below `re` at the end of the run, on the same formulas (5860 lines, each case's sequence twice, 704 with a proxy), no failure.  With `CondFresh` from the run's end to the final state this says that unproxying by the final `cond` is unproxying by the run's own `cond` on those blocks (`unproxy_condFresh`); the ops a later run puts in front of the run's lazy blocks are what `AgreeU`'s lazy-block clause takes unproxied.  Left for the runs: `AgreeU` in the `sem` of a relation that makes proxies and `CondFresh` in place of `cnd`; the present relations keep `AgreeP`, since `AgreeU` with a non-empty `cond` would need every block of a run without proxies to mention no proxy register, an invariant (the registers of the blocks a run emits are below its end, except the fill ops of the goal's entries) that nothing proves yet.  The `+collapse-lazy-atom` case needs a second change, and the earlier statement of it here was wrong about where `d` lies.  `FillSem` and `FrontSem` fill a lazy block from the input registers `ned` only, while the unproxied `%mov d r'` reads `d`.  In `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` (raw blocks of `tools/hoon/proxy-dump.hoon`, case `%h`, rerun 2026-11-24), `d` is raw `v3`, Gen register 2, the `rp` of the `%5` inside `q`: `q` allocates Gen 0 to 3 (`imm 5`, `don`, `rp`, `rq`), `p` then the proxy (Gen 4, gone after unproxying) and `arg` (Gen 5).  So `d` is a register of `q`'s own sure need, allocated in `q`'s run and below its end, and `collapseLazyAtomL` takes it as `r` because `q`'s sure need is `this d`.  A write bound of the form "a run writes only registers allocated in it and those of its goal's need" therefore does not keep `d`.  What keeps it is that a run's code does not write the registers of its own result's sure need (here the code of `q` is `imm 6 v4` and `eqq v3 v4`).  The route that fits: widen the input need of `q`'s `sem` to `pair ned sur` holding `[s0 s]`, with `sur` the sure need of `q`'s result.  The fill ops of the final pass at the other lazy blocks meet the widened `FillSem` (`FillSem.pair`, since `sur` lies below `q`'s end); the earlier objection to this route was wrong.  What blocks it is the hypothesis `∀ r, NMem ned r → g1.re ≤ r` of `RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem` and `RunOKE.sem`.  So the change is to relax it to `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` and to prove, case by case, that the ops a run emits do not write its own sure-need registers.  Tested on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/selfkeep.py`, 2026-11-24): over the 74 formulas of `thread-a.hoon` and the two of `proxy-dump.hoon`, 2930 runs of `run` in `%run` mode, 352 of which make a proxy, the ops a run emits (new blocks, ops put in front of old blocks, parameters written by new jumps) write no register of its result's sure need (974 runs have both a non-empty sure need and writes) nor of its lazy entries' needs, no register of a need of the result is a key of `cond` (834 runs have both a non-empty sure need and a non-empty `cond`), and the sure need and the entries' needs share no register; no failure. The lower bound is `AboveIn m lo g` (every block of `g` at a label in `[lo, g.bo)` mentions only registers from `m` on) with the invariant `KeepsA m` (every `AboveIn m lo` kept, `re` and `bo` grown, `drop` kept), under the hypotheses that the registers of the goal (its need and the arguments of its jumps, `GoalA m`) are at least `m` and that `m ≤ re`; the registers of the result are then at least `m` too (`NextA`, `NextAL`).  It holds of every case of `run`, because every register that an emitted block or a put op mentions is a register of the goal's need (`+mede`, `+split`, `+into`, `+copy`, the `%mov` and `crashNeed` of `+collapse-lazy-atom`), an argument of the goal's jumps (the `%brn`, `%eqq`, `%clq` under `%pick`) or a fresh register, which is at least `re` and so at least `m`, while the `%bom` blocks (of `[0 0]`, of cons under `%pick`, of `+collapse-lazy-atom`) mention none; and since the run does not drop, every block counted in `[lo, bo)` is one that `emit` put there, so `Clear` is not needed.  `run_keepsA` is `run_keeps` helper by helper with the bound turned around, and `runX_keepsA` is `runX_keeps` case by case, the `%6` cases of `runL` included (`keepXA_sixP`, and `keepXA_sixN`, where the three blocks of `+fork-sure` mention the fresh copies `s0`, `s1` and the goal's need), taking `KeepX` of the parts for the labels of the entries, their number and `cond`.  `aboveX_of_fragX` gives `AboveX p` for every `p` of `FragX`, so `finishLF_bellContract74X` covers `[7 [4 p] q]` and `finishLF_bellContract73X` covers `[7 [3 p] q]` for every `p` and `q` of `FragX`, at most 255 `%6`, nothing baked and a cape with no `[| |]`, the second still given `hq3`. For the linking track: no look set the model makes holds the axis `0`.  `+from-sure` makes looks `axe` (never `0`: `fromSure 0` is `none`) and `pegF axe x` of old looks (`fromSure_lok_ne0`), `+split-sure` crashes on a look at `0` and makes `mas` of looks from `4` on (`splitLok_ne0`), `+into-sure` crashes on one (`gep` of `0` is `none`) and makes old looks, `gep` results (`lowB ||| 2^k`, not `0`) and `axe ≥ 2`, and `+copy` and `+fork-sure` take unions and copies.  So the model's `pegF axe 0 = 0` is never reached, but the invariant over every run is not proved (an induction over `runL` with the look sets of goals and results), and the model's `fromSure` would follow the Hoon exactly with `none` when `0` is in `sur.lok`. Its obstacle is `+sect`: `sixNext_okN` uses `sect_spec`, which needs `cond` unchanged by the branches (`cQ`), and with proxy runs in the branches the threading `sectThread` folds `sectStep` over the keys the branches made (`made-0`, `made-1`).  Each step skips a key whose tag names the branches' region id `rid` (`tag.any (· == rid)`), and then the threading puts every block back and leaves `cond`.  In the model every proxy gets the tag of the block it is read in (`proxy`, through `g.tags o`), tags are set by the `%6` that makes the lazy blocks, to `rid' :: region` under `%next` (`+sect`) and to `region` under `%done` and `%pick` (`sixBody`), with `region` that of the run of that `%6`, and the branches run with the region `rid :: region`, so every proxy a branch under a fork-free goal makes carries `rid`. Baking with `hbake`: `+bake-need` not the identity, so the entry need is the baked one and the constants it fixes must be the subject's.  `SitesMeet` of `BakeImp.lean` was to give that and is false; `+bake-graph` before commit 8f2162e miscompiled (`VariantCollision.lean`).  With the guard of that commit every bake lies under its code requirement (`bakes_ok`, `bakesImply`, `InvB.varsSub`), so the contract of a variant is the plain `BellContract` on its bake, and `skaPokeB_sound` has no hypothesis about the bakes.

   9. **Calls: the design** (2026-11-24, eleventh session, `CompCall.lean`, `tools/hoon/calldump.hoon`).  The case `%2` of `+run` (lines 3900 to 4051 of `nock-compilation.hoon`): an indirect call is `+simple-next`, `+kerf` (a hop block and the product `pro` from `+kern`), a block `[%nok r-sub r-fol pro]` hopping to the `+kerf` block, then `q` for `this r-fol`, `+past`, `p` for `this r-sub`, `+copy`, never a tail call.  A direct call reads the callee's need (`+callee-need`: a hot jet's, `map-local` or `top` in the SCC, else the n-ary cache or a compile of the callee's SCC, which extends `cache`), allocates its registers (`+need-ord-alloc-regs`), and emits under `%done` a tail call `[%jmp b sub-v]`, otherwise `[%cal b sub-v pro]` into the block of `+kerf`; then `q` (skipped when `+safe-fol-fol`, as when the analysis made it `[1 battery]`), `+past`, `p` for the callee's need, `+copy`.  A pure callee whose product nothing needs is not called (a callee with a `%6`, or in a recursive SCC, is never pure).  Memo keys give `%cam`, `%csm`; a ring gives `%caf`, `%jmf`; `mono` (only the whole-subject version, `+compile-unary`) gives an optimistic call and a pessimistic one on the whole subject joined by `+mono-try-call`; `sof` (the need is not sound for `made p sup` and soft or hinted) records a soft call (`+soft-new`), whose need is bound at three blocks before the call (`+soft-bond`) and taken apart there after `+coerce-lazy` (`+soft-done`, `+soft-emit`).  The n-ary versions compile the nomm of `+inlined`, which inlines a callee alone in its SCC, not recursive, not hot, with no memo key, of at most 48 nodes: the raw blocks need a recursive callee to show a call.  Raw blocks (subject unknown, the recursive `R = [6 [3 [0 3]] [9 2 [10 [3 [0 7]] [0 1]]] [0 3]]`, need `[~ this]`): `[8 [1 R] [9 2 [0 1]]]` is `mov v0 v1` then `jmp R [v1]`; `[4 [8 [1 R] [9 2 [0 1]]]]` is `cal R [v3] v4`, `mov v4 v2`, `inc v2 v1, don v1`; for `%pick` `cal R [v3] v5`, `mov v5 v4`, `brn v4`; the indirect `[2 [0 3] [0 2]]` is `nok v4 v5 v3`, `mov v3 v2`, `don v2`.  The model: the state needs `soft`, `sofs` (`GenC`) only for soft calls; what the case reads of `cache` and the code table is `CallEnv`, functions of the callee (a compiled callee's need does not change within a poke).  `callL` models the indirect call, the dropped call and the direct call for `mono` `|`, no memo key, no ring, `sof` false, with the runs of `p` and `q` as parameters; it is exact there (the call block is fresh, so no `+soft-bond`, and `+kern` meets no soft block, as `kern` with `noSoft` assumes), and four `#guard` lines check it against the raw blocks (indirect for `%done`, tail call, `%next`, `%pick`; the registers are the raw ones less the number of arguments).  Done (twelfth session): `runLC` (`CompRunLC.lean`, generated by `tools/gen/runlc.py`), with `env.cod` for `+past` (a call of an impure callee is hinted: in `[[call] [0 1]]` the tail's need is bound behind the call, `0w3 mov v0 v3` after `0w5 cal`) and `sup` threaded as the Hoon does; `runLC_eq` (without `%2` it is `runL`, so the links and theorems about `runL` stay valid); nine `#guard` checks of `finishLFC` against whole functions (`CompRunLCEq.lean`), all equal to the raw blocks.  `runLC` is over `Gen`, not `GenC`: `callL` is (sof false never touches `soft`), so `GenC` waits for the soft calls.  The call blocks: `goJ_cal`, `callBlockSem`, `goJ_jmp`, `goJ_nok`, `allocSplit` (`CompCallSem.lean`); one call against the oracle: `callStep_fwd`, `tailStep_fwd` (`CompCallStep.lean`).  Two corrections to `SemC` (twelfth session, `CompCallStep.lean`).  First, the fuel: `SemC` takes `nommF` with fuel `n` and `OracleFwd` with the same `n`, but a callee's nomm may take fewer steps than `+mink` on its formula (`Annot.fold` makes a computation `[1 v]`), so the callee's `+mink` fuel is not below `n`; `+mink` of `[2 b c]` with fuel `j + 1` runs the callee with fuel `j`, so the relation must be indexed by `+mink`'s fuel on the caller's formula with `Annot` (`SemCM`).  Second, `GoalSemC` compares a crash with `Tone.two t` of the interpreter's trace, which `ToneRel` does not relate to itself when it holds a `%meaf` frame: `GoalSemCM` takes `+mink`'s trace `tm`.  The joint induction over a fragment with calls (the analogue of `UOK`, not written yet) needs per nomm and goal: the structural clauses of `UOK` as they are (for a part without `%2` they are `runL`'s through `runLC_eq`; for a call they come from `callL`'s blocks, which are fresh, and the runs of `p` and `q`), and the clause `SemCM` instead of the `sem` of `RunOKV`.  A pure part gets `SemCM` from its existing relation through `goalSemC_of_goalSem` (the state stays, `toneOf tm v`) and `nomm_bwd` (its `pureEval` is `+mink`'s product); a parent (cons, `%6`, `%7`, `%10`) composes `SemCM` of its parts with the state moving between them, which needs `AgreeU` at every state and `frames_mono` for the trace handed on; a direct call composes `SemCM` of `p` (the subject held as the callee's need) with `callStep_fwd` or `tailStep_fwd`, `Annot.direct` giving `Fits b.less` and `b.fol` from `q`.  Left for the first call theorem: the run of `p` must put the callee's subject in the registers of `+need-ord-alloc-regs` (the `%next` goal `lazy-from-need sub-ned`, a `RunOKV` of `p` for that goal), the product register `pro` and the `+kerf` block at `out` must meet the caller's goal (as `kerf` does for `%4`), and the `q` of `+safe-fol-fol` must be shown not to run.  Features of calls not modelled, with what a first end-to-end theorem about a program with one call needs: (a) `+inlined` (line 3298): a pass before `+comp`, whose output carries `Annot.inline`; not needed in the model of `+comp`, which takes the nomm it receives. (b) Memo calls (`%cam`, `%csm`, lines 3973 to 3980; `b-ned` is `this` for a persistent key, line 3948): only under a `%memo` hint; not needed. (c) Ring calls (`%caf`, `%jmf`, `%csf`, `%jsf`, `rin` at line 3939): a callee that is a registered jet; the interpreter runs them as plain calls, so the lemmas of `CompCallSem.lean` carry over unchanged; not needed. (e) Soft calls (`sof`, lines 3963 to 3970, `+soft-new` 5190, `+soft-bond` 5206, `+soft-ask` 5221, `+soft-done` 5286, `+soft-emit` 5311, `+sound` 5173, `+need-soft` 7675): `sof` is false in every dump so far (the nine whole functions match with `sof` false); needed only when `+sound` fails on a soft or hinted call, so a first theorem can avoid them, but `env.sof` must then be shown false. (f) Hot jets (`+callee-need` line 4593 takes a hot jet's need, `b-sur` is `[%none ~]`): not needed for a callee without a hot jet. (g) The SCC fixed point (`map-local`, `top`, item 7): needed for a recursive callee such as `R`, whose own code is compiled against its SCC; a first theorem avoids it with a callee that does not recur and has more than 48 nodes (else `+inlined` removes the call).    10. **The whole-subject version** (2026-11-24, thirteenth session, `CompWhole.lean`, `CompWholeKern.lean`, `CompWholeTop.lean`, `tools/hoon/wholedump.hoon`).  `+poke` answers `%full` with `+compiled-mono` (line 9683, after `+optimize`), `%dire b &` with `+compiled-mono` and `%dire b |` with `+compiled-nary` (lines 9694, 9695).  `+compiled-mono` is `+compile-unary` (line 3390): `run` with `mono` `&` for `[%done ~]`, then `+kerf` (a new hop block to the run's entry, and `+kern` of the run's lazy need from one fresh register `sub`, through proxies at the lazy blocks other than the new one), then `+to-straight` of `[%next [[this+sub ~] ~ ~] ~ o]` at the new block.    14. **The indirect call under the goals of a parent** (2026-11-24, seventeenth session, `CompIndirectOut.lean`, `CompCallParent.lean`).  Proved: `indirect_runS` (and its corollary `indirect_runN`): the run of `[2 p q]` with `p`, `q` meeting `UOK`, at most 255 `%6` and a formula with `MinkN2`, under a fork-free `%next sur th` with `th` argless, from any state with the hypotheses of `OKNV` (`GJ`, `GoalOK`, `1 ≤ bo`, no drop, `UpBelow`, `Clear`, `CondBelow`, no register of `sur` a key of `cond`), meets `RunOKNC`: every structural field of `RunOKNV`, `nec` on the pure pair `[p q]` (a subject that does not fit the need makes `[p q]` crash, so `+mink` crashes by `MinkN2`), and `SemNC`, which is `SemNV` with `GoalSemCM` against an oracle with `OracleSteps` and `OracleFwd n` for `+mink`'s tone with fuel `n` on the formula, keeping `(x < g0.re ∨ NMem ned x) ∧ ¬ NMem sur.ned x` as `SemNV` does.  `indirect_runD` and `indirect_runP` do the same under `%done` and `%pick z o`: the block of `+simple-next` (`[don re]` or `[brn re z o]` at `bo`) is kept to the end, and the rest of the run meets `RunOKNC` under `%next (lazy-from-reg re)` into it from the state after that block.  `outGoal_done` and `outGoal_pick` turn `OutGoal` for that `%next` into `OutGoal` for `%done` and `%pick`.  The pieces: `kerf_ofSure` (under a fork-free `%next` the lazy need has no entries, so `+kern` walks the block `out` only, with `pro` itself as its source and no proxy, and the block is the ops of `+kern-need` then `hop th`), `outGoal_kerf` (the block meets `OutGoal` in every program whose block at `out` is it unproxied by a `cond` without its registers as keys), `indirect_prefixN` (from any state the blocks before the runs grow the state with `GrowU` and keep `cond`).  `KernSemS` keeps only the registers below the ops' fresh ones, while a parent needs the registers of `ned` kept too, and those lie above every register of the run: in the raw blocks of `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]` the tail's block `0w3 mov v0 v3` reads the subject register `v0` after the call's blocks `0w5 nok v5 v6 v4` and `0w4 mov v4 v2`.  `runs_keep` gives that: ops of `+kern-need` (`KOp`: moves, `cel`, `hed`, `tal`, `kernLoop_kop`) keep every register they do not write, read through a `%don` after the ops because `Runs` is an equation of the interpreter.  Raw blocks: `CompCallParent.lean` checks `finishLUC` by `#guard` against `tools/hoon/wholedump.hoon` with the cases `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`, `[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` and `[7 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]` on the subject `[[4 0 1] 5]` (the compiler's products `[6 [4 0 1] 5]`, `[[[4 0 1] 5] 6]` and `6`), all equal.  `[7 [0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` is the call alone after the analysis.  Left, in order.  First, the cons with a call in its head under `%done` (`binaryGV_ok` with the head's `RunOKNV` replaced by `RunOKNC`): the copy block as there, then the head's `GoalSemCM` gives a state `st'` and registers keeping the cons's `K`, then the tail's `SemNV` at `st'` (so the `AgreeU` the cons takes must hold at every state, which `finishTop_gen` gives since it proves `AgreeU` for each state), then the `con` block, with `goalSemCM_step` and `goalSemC_of_goalSem` for the pure steps, and `+mink` on a cell formula runs the head first, so its crash is the cons's.  The top then follows from `finishTop_gen` with `Φ` the `ReturnsC` of the `%done` goal turned into `fwdPhi` and `bwdPhi` as `indirectTop_fwd` and `indirectTop_bwd` do.  Second, the mirror case (the call in the tail runs first at run time, so the head's pure `SemNV` is used at the call's new state) and `[7 p q]` (a call in `p` runs before `q`'s code, a call in `q` after `p`'s).  Third, `UOKC`, the joint induction with `GoalSemCM` clauses, whose leaves are `UOK` lifted by `goalSemC_of_goalSem` and whose call case is `indirect_runN`, `indirect_runD`, `indirect_runP`.  Eighteenth session (12.15): the first step is done for the forward half (`consCHTop_fwd`); `RunOKNC` changed (`SemNC` takes `AgreeU` at every state, no `size`, entries' registers may be the goal's), and the second step's description above of the mirror case is corrected in 12.15.
   15. **The cons with the call in its head** (2026-11-24, eighteenth session, `CompConsCall.lean`, `CompConsCallTop.lean`, `tools/gen/conscall.py`, `tools/gen/topall.py`).  Proved: `consCHTop_fwd` (and `consCHTop_fwdA` on `Annot`, `consCH_example`): the whole-subject code (`finishLUC env`) of the nomm `[[2 b c] d]` for a formula `[fh ft]`, with `b`, `c`, `d` meeting `UOK` (for example in `FragU`), at most 255 `%6`, `fh` a cell with `MinkN2` (any `Annot` of `[2 b c]` but the bare ones) and `ft` whose `+mink` is `d`'s value, has the need `this` and meets `ContractFwd` on every subject that fits the bell.  The backward half is not proved: no backward relation exists for a call under a parent (`indirect_semB` is a `Φ` at the top only), and `BellContract` needs both halves, so `RawMono` is not extended.  The run (`consCH_runN`): `+split`, the tail `d`, then `+past` behind the call (`pastL_hinted`: the call is hinted, so a tail need that asks something is parked in a bond behind a new `[hop]` block, which `+kern` later fills from the subject, `0w3 mov v0 v3` in the raw blocks; a need that asks nothing is left), the call (`indirect_runN`), `+copy`.  At run time the call runs first, then the block of `+past` fills the tail's need at the oracle's new state, so the subject is checked against the tail's need only after the call, as `+mink` (head first) asks.  `consNC_ok` (generated from `binaryGV_ok`) composes: the copy block, the head's `GoalSemCM`, the tail's `SemNV` at the new state (`TailNV`: `RunOKNV` with the block of `+past` among the entries, `tailNV_past`), the `con` block (`goalSemC_of_goalSem`, `goalSemCM_step`); `minkF_consC` is `+mink` on the cell formula.  The top: `finishLUC_fwdC` (any nomm whose run for `%done` is `[don 0]` and `RunOKNC` into it), through `finishTop_genA` (`finishTop_gen` with `AgreeU` at every state, which its proof gives since the ops of `+kern` do not touch the oracle).  Changes to `RunOKNC`: `SemNC` takes `AgreeU` at every state (the lazy blocks of a parent run after the call, at a new state), the field `size` is gone (the block of `+past` is one more entry than the `%6` count allows, and no theorem reads the bound), the entries' registers may be the goal's (`+split` of a pair goal gives the tail the goal's own registers, which the block of `+past` then fills), and `nec` speaks of the head's pure part (`[b c]` for the cons).  A correction to 12.14 on the mirror case `[[p] [2 ..]]`: in the raw blocks of `[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` the block `0w3 mov v0 v2, hop 2` is the `+past` of the head's need behind the call (the call in the tail is hinted), so at run time the head's code runs first, then the call, then the head's need is filled.  Left: the backward relation of the call under a parent (a `GoalSemCM` for `OracleBwd`: every result of the code from the jump is related to a `+mink` result), then `BellContract` of the cons; the mirror case (the `+past` of the head behind the call); `[7 p q]`; `UOKC`.  Nineteenth session (12.16): the backward half is proved (`consCHTop_bwd`, `consCHTop_contract`), on every `Annot` of the nomm (`consCH_contract_of_annot`), and `RawMono` holds on `FragUCCM`.
   16. **The backward relation of code that calls** (2026-11-24, nineteenth session, `CompIndirectOutB.lean`, `CompConsCallB.lean`, `CompConsCallTopB.lean`, `CompConsCallWrap.lean`, `CompHypOfCons.lean`, `tools/gen/conscallb.py`).  The design: two relations with one core, not one "iff" relation.  The forward relation plus determinism does not give the backward one: an oracle with `OracleBwd` need not answer where `+mink` returns (the oracle that never answers has `OracleBwd` and no `OracleFwd n`), so `SemNC` cannot be applied to it, and extending it with `+mink`'s answers needs an interpreter tone related to every `+mink` tone, which `ToneRel` does not give (a `%mean` frame whose trap is neither a bell's call nor `+mack`s has no related frame), plus the monotonicity of the interpreter in the oracle; and the case where `+mink` does not halt (the code must not return) is the whole backward induction anyway.  On `[2 b c]` the backward step is direct: the code returned, so the oracle answered the `%nok` on the products `x`, `y`, `OracleBwd.nok` gives `+mink` on `[x y]`, `MinkN2.bwd` makes it a run on the formula, and the block `out` gives the rest as forward.  So `SemNCB` (the counterpart of `SemNC`, same hypotheses with `OracleBwd` and no fuel) says: if the code from the run's jump returns anything, there is `r` with `Mink lib gul tm s f r` and `GoalSemCM` at `r`, the same predicate as forward, so a parent's step from the head's `GoalSemCM` at `rh` to its own at `r` is the forward text (`consNC_bwd` is `consNC_ok` with only the head's tone and the cons's `+mink` run changed, generated).  Proved: `indirect_semGB`, `indirect_runSB`, `indirect_runNB`, `indirect_runDB`, `indirect_runPB` (the indirect call under a fork-free `%next`, `%done`, `%pick`, the hypotheses of `indirect_runS`), `finishLUC_bwdC` (`ContractBwd` of the whole-subject code from `RunOKNC` and `SemNCB` under `%done`), `indirectTop_bwdC` and `indirectTop_bwd'` (the check: `indirectTop_bwd` re-derived, on any formula with `MinkN2`), `mink_consC_bwd`, `consNC_bwd`, `consCH_runNB`, `consCH_runDB`, `consCHTop_bwd`, `consCHTop_contract` (`BellContract` of `[[2 b c] d]`, the tail's `+mink` given in both directions, `Mq` and `MqB`), `consCHTop_contractA` and `consCH_exampleC` (`[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`, both halves).  The wrappers: `RawMono` quantifies over every `Annot` of the nomm, and `Annot.memo`, `Annot.six0`, `Annot.six1` wrap any nomm, so `consCH_contract_of_annot` goes through them with `WrapRel` (`+mink` on the wrapper runs the inner formula with no more fuel and returns what it returns, `contract_wrap`).  So `RawMono` holds on `FragUCCM` = `FragUM` or `IndUM` or `ConsUM` (`[[2 b c] d]` with `b`, `c`, `d` in `FragU`, at most 255 `%6`, `bareLitQ c = false`), `rawMono_fragUCC`, and `compHyp_of_fragCC` derives `CompHyp` from it.  `RunOKNC` is unchanged: the backward relation is a separate `SemNCB` beside it (a field would have forced every producer to change at once).  Left: the mirror case (the call in the tail, with the head's need filled behind the call by `+past`) and `[7 p q]` with the call in `p`, both halves; then `UOKC` with the clauses `RunOKNC` and `SemNCB`.  Twentieth session (12.17): `UOKC` carries both clauses in one relation, `RunOKGC`, with `RunOKNC.nec` replaced by the semantic `NecC`.
   17. **The joint induction for code that calls** (2026-11-24, twentieth session, `CompUOKC.lean`, `CompConsGH.lean`, `CompFragUC.lean`, `CompHypOfUOKC.lean`, `tools/gen/consokf.py`, `tools/hoon/cc2dump.hoon`).  The relation of one run, for any goal: `RunOKGC lib P f goal g0 g1 nex`, the structural fields of `RunOKNC` (with `GMem goal` for the goal's registers), `nec`, `sem` (`SemGC`, which under `%next` is `SemNC` itself) and `semB` (`SemGCB`, under `%next` `SemNCB`).  One clause had to change: `RunOKNC.nec` says that a pure part `pq` has no value where the subject does not fit the run's need, which a call-free part does not meet (`[0 1]` under a goal that asks a cell has a value on an atom, a value that does not fit the goal), so `RunOKGC.nec` is semantic: `NecC lib f goal s`, every `+mink` result on `[s f]` is a crash with the caller's trace or a product that does not fit the goal (`BadC`), and there is one.  The clauses of a part, `UOKC lib env P f nm`: `kind` (the part is call-free, with `UOK` and `MinkEq`, `+mink` on `f` its pure value, which a parent uses for the call-free relations; or it is hinted, so `+past` parks a need that asks something behind it, `pastL_hinted`), `clean` (`CleanA` for `runLC`) and `run` (`OKCN`: every run from a state with the hypotheses of `OKNV` meets `RunOKGC`).  Both `clean` and `run` are asked for every goal but `%pick` (`NoPick`): the leaves meet the whole `OKC` (`okc_of_uok`, `okc_indirect`), but the cons whose head calls runs under `%pick` as a `bom` block with both parts under `%next bunt`, which needs `binaryGV_ok`'s pick case with a calling head, not done.  The tag clauses (`TagNV`, `TagV`) are read only by `%6` under `%next` and are not restated.  Proved.  The lift (`uokc_of_uok`): `RunOKNV.toGC`, `RunOKV.toGC` with `goalSemC_of_goalSem` (the state stays), backward with `+mink`'s result the pure value.  The call (`uokc_indirect`): `indirect_runN` and `indirect_runNB` under `%next`; under `%done` and `%pick` the block of `+simple-next` at `bo` then the run under `%next (lazy-from-reg re)` into it, turned into the goal by `runOKGC_of_next` (`goalSemCM_done_of_next`, `goalSemCM_pick_of_next`); `nec` from `MinkN2` (`minkN2_crashes`).  The wrappers (`UOKC.wrap` through `WrapRel`, for `Annot.memo`, `Annot.six0`, `Annot.six1`).  The top (`finishLUC_contractG`): the `%done` run of any nomm with `UOKC` gives `TopS`, and `finishTop_genA` with `fwdPhi`, `bwdPhi` gives both halves, without the `[don 0]` shape that `finishLUC_fwdC` asks.  The first parent (`uokc_consH`, `CompConsGH.lean`): the cons `[p d]` with `p` hinted with `UOKC`, `d` with `UOK` and `MinkEq`, for `[fh ft]` with `fh` a cell, under `%next` (`consGH_runN`: the run of `consCH_runN` with `p`'s `RunOKGC` in place of `indirect_runN`) and `%done` (`consGH_runD`).  `consNC_ok` and `consNC_bwd` are reused unchanged: the head is passed as a `RunOKNC` with the pure part `[0 0]` (`RunOKGC.toNC0`, its `nec` holds of every subject), and the cons's `NecC` is made from the head's (`necC_consH`) through the fit of the head's need, which `consNC_okF` (`consNC_ok` with one more conclusion, generated by `tools/gen/consokf.py`) returns.  The fragment: `FragC` (`ind`: `[2 p q]` with `p`, `q` in `FragU` and `bareLitQ q = false`; `consH`: `[p d]` with `p` in `FragC`, `d` in `FragU`), `FragUC` = `FragU` or `FragC`, `uokc_of_fragUC` (every `Annot` of a nomm of `FragUC` meets `UOKC`), `finishLUC_contractUC` (`BellContract … true` on `FragUC` with at most 255 `%6`), `rawMono_fragUCG` (`RawMono` on `FragUCG`), `fragUCCM_sub`, `compHyp_of_fragUCG`.  Example: `[[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]] [0 1]]` (`uokc_example2`), whose `finishLUC` equals the raw blocks by `#guard` (the outer tail's need is parked behind the inner cons, `0w3 mov v0 v3`, and the inner tail's behind the call, `0w5 mov v0 v5`).  Not in the fragment, and why.  The cons with a call in its tail (`[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`) or in both parts (`[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`): the tail is hinted, so `+past` parks the head's need `hed` behind it at the split (`nexh = ⟨mk bunt [] [(o, mk hed [] [])], jmp0 o⟩`), and the head runs for `%next nexh.laz th`, a goal whose lazy need has a bond, while every relation so far (`OKNV`, `UOK`, `UOKC`) is about goals `%next (ofSure sur) th`.  So `UOKC.run` must cover goals `%next L th` with `L` a bunt with one bond entry (the block at `o` fills `hed` from the head's product after the tail's code ran), for call-free heads too; that is the clause to strengthen before the mirror case, and it needs a `GoalSem` for a lazy goal with entries.  `[7 p q]` (`[7 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`): `p` runs for `q`'s result need, which has entries when `q` has a `%6`, the same issue; for `q` without `%6` (`Frag q`, as `uok_seven` asks) `p` runs for a goal `%next (ofSure ..)` (the shape `runOKNV_n7` hands `OKNV`), so `[7 p q]` with a call in `p` and `q` in `Frag` is the next constructor to try with `UOKC` as it is.  So `RawMono` holds on `FragUCG`.
   18. **Goals with entries: one relation** (2026-11-24, twenty-first session, `CompLazyGoal.lean`, `tools/hoon/lazydump.hoon`).  The raw blocks first.  In `[[4 [0 3]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` the bond block of `+past` is `0w3: mov v7 v2, hop 0w2`, and `v7` is written by `0w7: inc v9 v7`, which runs before the call `0w5: nok v5 v6 v4`.  So the ops that a run puts at a lazy block of its goal read registers that the run computed, and every code between the run's end and the lazy block must keep them.  In `[[0 1] ..]` and `[[0 2] ..]` the head keeps the bond entry at its label and the final pass fills it from `v0`, and in `[7 [[0 2] [0 3]] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [1 0]]]` the tail of the cons keeps the fork entry's half `this v2` at the branch block, which the final pass fills.  The relation: `RunOKL lib nm L th D W g0 g1 nex`, the structural fields of `RunOKE` with `CondFresh`, a need `D s` with a noun `W s` for each subject `s` (the registers the run computed for its lazy blocks, allocated in the run), `front` (every goal entry got ops `F` in front of its block, and after any ops `A` that fill the result's entry at that label, `A ++ F` unproxied fills the goal entry for the product from the input need `pair ned (D s)` holding `[s0 (W s)]`, `FrontL`), and `sem` (`SemNV` with `AgreeL`, the agreement at the result entries the run made, and with `D s` holding `W s` at `th`, `GoalSemD`).  The parent keeps `D s` until the lazy block by giving the code that runs in between the input need `pair ned (D s)`, as `runOKNV_seven4` did for one register.  What it keeps of the earlier relations: of `RunOKE` the pairing of goal and result entries at the same label, of `FrontSemR` the shape of the front, and of `CollRunU` the `sem` against every later `cond` and the extra input register.  Proved: `RunOKL.toNV` and `RunOKNV.toL` (for a goal with no entries it is `RunOKNV`, with `D = none`), `[0 axe]` (`runOKL_n0`, and `runOKL_n0_run` from the run), `[4 p]` from `CollRunU` with `D s = this d` (`runOKL_n4`, and `runOKL_n4_none` from the run when the goal's sure part asks nothing, as behind a call), and the composition `[7 p q]` (`runOKL_seven`, `D s = pair (Dp s) (Dq (p s))`), which shows that the relation composes.  Not done: `[1 k]`, `[3 p]`, `%5`, `%10` and the cons, the cons with a call in its tail and `[7 p q]` with a `%6` in `q` in general.  Where it stops, with the raw blocks.  First, the cons at a goal entry whose need is `this r` or `both r ..`: `+split` puts `con h t r` at the entry's block and keeps both halves at that label, so a result entry's block is not empty, which `labs` and `AgreeL` ask.  The weakest change is `AgreeL` with a body (`g1` holds `[[] B [%hop th]]` at the entry, the program `A ++ B` unproxied, `A` filling the entry), with `front` about the prefix that the run put before the parent's body, as now.  Second, the `sem` is strict, so `D s` must be allocated in the run.  In `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` the goal's sure part is the register `d` of the `%5`, which `+collapse-lazy-atom` reads in the branch blocks, and the `%5`'s code must keep its own input register `d`, which only the relaxed form (`InR`, `KeepI` of `CollRunU`) says, and the relaxed `sem` is proved over `FragX` but not over `FragU`.  Third, under `+fork` the ops of a branch in a kid of a lazy block read that branch's registers only on its path, which is why `D` depends on the subject.  For the cons with a call in its tail the head needs only the strict form: its bond goal's sure part is the bunt (`runOKL_n4_none` gives the head `[4 [0 3]]` of the example), and the call keeps `D s` because `D s` lies above the call's end.  What is left there is the composition itself, with `copyL` and the call's `RunOKGC` in place of `q`'s `RunOKL` in `runOKL_seven`.
